The existing validator trigger is `BEFORE INSERT` on `public.refunds`. | Existing RPC definitions remain; validator behavior changes for refund inserts. Production `pg_cron` is installed, but this review did not inspect its live job rows. | Any privileged writer may remain able to write; service-role grants are unchanged. | **UNKNOWN.** Local source and deployment metadata cannot establish the inventory of separately managed integrations or current database jobs. |

### Permission-change conclusion

**No confirmed application regression was found in the identified accepted-DEV or existing RPC paths.** The previous Production preflight found RLS enabled, FORCE RLS off, and only an authenticated SELECT policy, so ordinary `anon`/`authenticated` INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE were already rejected by RLS. The reviewed migration removes those table grants and removes TRUNCATE privileges, which RLS does not govern, while preserving SELECT and service-role privileges. The grant revocation therefore does not appear to remove a successful ordinary client DML path in the inspected policy state; it is a security tightening. That conclusion is bounded to the preflight state and does not rule out an external wrapper, future policy, service-role writer, or unreviewed deployed application path.

**Remaining behavioral risk:** The replacement validator and expanded constraints affect legacy RPC and privileged service-role inserts even though their function grants are preserved. Known legacy overpayment, Fresh Pick cancellation, payment-correction, and V1 paths passed the recorded isolated tests. Any unlisted direct service-role insert or external SQL writer may see a changed validation result. No source evidence establishes that such a writer exists, and no absence claim is made.

**Production deployment compatibility — UNRESOLVED:** The identified application paths are compatible in the reviewed DEV/isolated test scope, but exact deployed Production code, service-role integrations, operational-script execution history, and live `pg_cron` job rows are unknown. The local test cleanup scripts and DEV cleanup SQL are not evidence that these paths run in Production. Do not run them against Production; keep the migration security revocations intact.

**Can the unknowns be bounded? — YES, with a narrow inventory:** Without restarting historical source recovery, identify the owner-designated maintained source/build for the current application and inspect only refund-related callers; list active Production database scheduled jobs and configured Edge Functions/integrations that could issue refund DML/RPCs; and inspect the deployment/operations repositories for refund mutation scripts. Confirm writer identity/path and role without reading business rows or secrets. This inventory is still required before migration execution. The recovery/operational gate remains separately unresolved.

**Decision:** No confirmed regression from the reviewed permission changes was identified in the available application/RPC paths. Production compatibility remains **UNKNOWN** for the live app and outside service-role writers, so the migration is **not cleared for execution**. The reviewed migration, legacy RPCs, service-role permissions, and Production data remain unchanged.

**One next executable step:** Perform a bounded, read-only inventory of the current Production refund-writing callers—maintained application source, active database jobs/Edge Functions, and operational integrations/scripts—then classify each by RPC versus direct DML and execution role. Do not execute the migration or any refund operation.

**Target — VERIFIED:** Read-only project metadata identified `cuowwlduhxxifmpvpltt` as `project-flour-prod`, ACTIVE_HEALTHY, PostgreSQL 17.6. No Production data rows were queried. The reviewed migration was not executed; no deployment/configuration/permission change was made.

The called SECURITY DEFINER functions and their directly referenced public helper functions were inspected using sanitized catalog-derived indicators; none referenced refunds or contained refund DML. **Refund trigger — VERIFIED:** `refunds_assert_within_overpayment` is a `BEFORE INSERT` trigger on `public.refunds`; its function is `assert_refund_within_overpayment`, owned by `postgres`, SECURITY DEFINER. The trigger function contains refund validation references but no direct refund-table DML. It is a validator, not an independent writer. The reviewed migration replaces its validation behavior; known legacy inserts passed isolated tests. ## Refund Recording V1 final Production release-readiness decision — 16 October 2026

**Decision: OPTION C — DEFER BOTH changes until the concrete safety prerequisites below are met.** This section supersedes earlier next-step suggestions to repeat the caller inventory: active Production cron jobs and Supabase Edge Functions have now been inspected, and the remaining application/external caller uncertainty is recorded below. No tests or audits were repeated for this decision.

### Remaining risks and release effect

| Risk | Potential impact | Mitigating evidence | Migration gate | Application deployment gate | Smallest risk-reducing action |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A. Deployed application refund calls UNKNOWN** | A hidden direct/service-role writer could encounter changed trigger validation; unrecognized client path could behave differently. | Exact deployment source remains unavailable. Existing client-role INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE were already blocked by RLS; known legacy RPCs are unchanged and passed isolated tests. | **Blocks for now** because call path through privileged writes is unknown. | **Blocks** until an owner-designated maintained candidate is reviewed; exact historical parity remains unavailable. | Obtain an owner/maintainer statement of current Production refund UI/action routes and their RPC/role, using existing operational knowledge; do not resume source recovery. |
| **B. External/service-role writers UNKNOWN** | Privileged direct inserts bypass RLS and still invoke the new validator/constraints; a writer may receive new validation errors. | `service_role` permissions are preserved. No refund-writing cron or Supabase Edge Function was found. Local direct-delete scripts are test/DEV-only evidence, not proof of Production execution. | **Blocks** until active external/operational writers are enumerated or explicitly accepted as absent by their owners. | **Blocks** if those integrations must continue during/after the app release. | Get a bounded owner attestation/inventory of separately managed payment/refund integrations and scripts, including whether they write directly or via RPC. |
| **C. New refund validator and constraints** | A current legacy refund row or privileged insert outside tested categories could fail validation or violate the expanded constraints. | Existing refund types remain allowed; metadata is nullable; isolated migration applied to October 14 restored rows; known legacy/V1/payment-correction tests passed. Current post-October-14 refund rows were not checked against the draft predicates. | **Conditional blocker:** require one read-only aggregate compatibility check on current refund rows immediately before any execution. | App requires the migration before calling V1 fields/RPCs and focused tests against the final release candidate. | Prepare a count-only SELECT that detects rows incompatible with the reviewed constraints, with no customer values returned. |
| **D. Full disaster recovery incomplete** | Failure could leave current orders, payments/refunds, Fresh Pick stock, accounts, catalogue/pricing, or assets unrecoverable. October 14 restore is stale and partial. | Existing encrypted backup is verified and useful for historical isolated testing only; partial restore exposed missing Supabase-managed components/ownership/ACLs. | **Blocks.** | **Blocks.** | Verify the currently available Supabase point-in-time recovery/restore capability and a current-state-preserving recovery runbook, including an isolated restore path; do not restore the October 14 snapshot over live Production. |
| **E. Prior Production timeouts/aborted transactions** | Migration locks or app traffic during recurrence could worsen availability or interrupt workflows. | Incident recovered; root cause remains unknown. Current cron/Edge inventory found no refund job. | **Conditional stop:** proceed only during a healthy window with a short execution/monitoring plan; recurrence blocks immediately. | **Conditional stop:** healthy checks and monitoring required; recurrence blocks release. | At the release window, verify normal health and define a stop/abort response; no broad incident investigation is indicated absent recurrence. |
| **F. Exact application-source parity unavailable** | A replacement could alter unrelated storefront, staff, checkout, payment, inventory, or report behavior. | Historical source recovery is exhausted; accepted DEV and preserved Production-oriented sources are available but not exact deployed source. | **Does not by itself invalidate a backward-compatible schema change**, but current caller uncertainty and recovery risk still block migration now. | **Blocks a claim of exact replacement/parity.** A bounded candidate can only proceed from an owner-designated maintained source with explicit acceptance that historical parity is unknown and broad regression results. | Owner designates the maintained source tree to use for the new release; do not search for or claim the missing deployment source. |

### Options

- **A — Migration first, application later:** Not recommended now. Known legacy RPCs and reads are designed to remain compatible, but active privileged direct writers are not fully known, current rows have not been checked against the draft predicates, and recovery is incomplete. The ordinary client DML revocation is not itself a confirmed regression because the existing SELECT-only RLS policy already denied client INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE.
- **B — Migration and application together:** Not ready now. Pairing removes the long schema/app gap but cannot cure the unknown deployed call sites, missing exact baseline, external service-role uncertainty, or recovery gap.
- **C — Defer both until prerequisites are satisfied:** **Recommended.** Keep the migration unchanged and unapplied; do not deploy V1 application code ahead of it.

### Minimum recovery safeguards before changing Production

- Verify a current, data-preserving database recovery capability (provider PITR or an equivalent current snapshot) that can restore orders, statuses, payments, allocations, refunds, inventory/reservations, staff/customer accounts, catalogues/prices, and Production-specific functions without replacing live data with DEV or an October 14 snapshot. Rehearse recovery into an isolated target or otherwise obtain verifiable provider evidence.
- Account separately for Auth/project configuration, staff passkeys/auth state, Storage object bytes, database roles/grants/extensions/jobs, and deployment configuration; a PostgreSQL dump alone does not restore the full Supabase application environment.
- Preserve a known-good application deployment/artifact and its configuration for application rollback. Application rollback does not revert the migration; prefer retaining the additive schema and rolling back app code, with a reviewed forward repair path that preserves any new refunds.
- Define who can stop the release, the monitoring window, migration lock/timeout expectations, and a current-state-preserving recovery response. Never restore the October 14 backup over current Production.

**Conditions before requesting Production execution authorization:** (1) owner-designated maintained application baseline and bounded refund-call-path statement; (2) external/service-role writer inventory with no unresolved incompatible path, or explicit owner acceptance of a named residual risk; (3) read-only current-row compatibility check passes; (4) current recovery safeguards above are verified and accepted; (5) healthy Production window and bounded lock/monitor plan; (6) immediate preflight still confirms project identity, exact migration SHA-256, migration version absent, and no relevant schema/ACL/RLS drift; and (7) the application candidate has been reviewed to preserve payment correction, both checkout overloads, legacy refund paths, Ready Override, and all live business data. If exact historical source remains unavailable, the release must be described as a replacement from the designated maintained baseline, not as parity with the October 13 deployment.

**One concrete next action:** Verify the current Production recovery capability and document the current-data-preserving restore/rollback runbook (database plus Auth, Storage, and deployment artifacts); do not create a new backup, restore anything, apply the migration, or deploy as part of this step.

Restore used `--no-owner --no-acl`; exact Production ownership and grants were not reconstructed. This October 14 snapshot is stale and is not a current-data recovery point. |

Project-specific backup/PITR status was not exposed by the read-only metadata available here. Previous read-only Production catalog checks verified selected current functions and permissions at their inspection time, not a complete restorable image of the current database. |
| Auth and passkeys | **PARTIALLY VERIFIED / UNVERIFIED** | Auth schema structures were present in the archive and local restore. Project-level Auth configuration (providers, redirect settings, SMTP/secrets and related settings) is outside that database snapshot. End-to-end recovery of staff identities, sessions, and passkey credentials has not been demonstrated. |

This verifies that historical archive, not completeness against current Storage. A database dump does not include the object bytes. |

| Vercel application artifact | **PARTIALLY VERIFIED** | Read-only deployment metadata returned current Production deployment `dpl_9toGsEsDYhYHn17takiszAjxRgjy` as `READY`, target `production`, source `CLI`, framework Next.js. The exact source commit is absent, so a source rebuild cannot be assumed equivalent. A read-only request to list rollback candidates was denied with Vercel API `403`; no rollback candidate was verified. No rollback or deployment action was taken. |
| Full current-state disaster recovery | **UNAVAILABLE / NOT VERIFIED** | No end-to-end restore of current Production—including business records, grants/ownership, Auth configuration, Storage bytes, managed dependencies, and application configuration—has been demonstrated. The October 14 backup cannot cover subsequent business changes. |

**Decision:** Recovery readiness is **insufficient to support a Production change**. The historical database and Storage backups are useful evidence and isolated test inputs, but neither establishes a current, complete recovery point. The available Vercel artifact is identified and READY, but exact source and rollback-candidate availability remain unverified. Do not represent project health metadata as a successful restore test.

### Operational response runbook

Use this as a decision sequence if a Production failure occurs. It is operational guidance only; do not perform destructive recovery steps without a separate, explicit owner decision.

1. **Declare and contain.** Record UTC and Malaysia timestamps, affected route/workflow, sanitized request/error IDs, and the first observed symptom. Check Supabase and Vercel service status and current deployment identity using read-only views. If errors are recurring (including statement timeout or aborted-transaction symptoms), stop release activity immediately. Do not retry refunds, payments, checkout, or migrations blindly; do not broaden permissions or disable RLS.
2. **Separate application from database symptoms.** If the database remains healthy and the issue follows an application deployment, preserve logs and deployment identity. An application rollback may be considered only to a known READY deployment with an independently verified artifact and explicit release-owner authorization. Do not assume such a candidate exists: the current rollback-candidate listing was blocked. Application rollback does not undo database changes.
3. **If a migration is in progress or has failed, stop.** Do not rerun it blindly. Establish whether its transaction committed and inspect the migration version and affected objects read-only. If atomic rollback is proven and the old application remains healthy, keep the old application and escalate the sanitized error. If commit state or partial effects are uncertain, do not attempt a dump restore or hand-written rollback; require a reviewed, data-preserving forward repair plan and owner authorization.
4. **If existing workflows fail after a committed schema change, preserve records.** Pause further release actions, capture the failing operation and request IDs, and use the known-good application artifact only if it is verified and authorized. Leave additive schema in place unless a reviewed rollback proves safe for newly created records. Prefer a compensating forward fix over dropping columns, constraints, functions, or new refund records.
5. **If data recovery is required, protect transactions after the recovery point.** Never restore the October 14 snapshot over live Production. First verify the provider's current recovery point, timestamp, retention, target-isolation options, and expected data loss window. A point-in-time restore to an earlier time can omit subsequent orders/payments/refunds; identify and preserve those transactions before any cutover. Restore into an isolated target where supported, validate aggregates and critical workflows, and obtain explicit owner approval before any production routing or replacement decision.
6. **Recover the whole service, not only PostgreSQL.** Track database schema/data and privileges, Auth identities/configuration/passkeys, Storage metadata and object bytes, managed extensions/jobs, secrets/provider settings, and the application artifact/configuration as separate recovery components. Verify each component before declaring service restored.
7. **Resume only after owner-led validation.** Verify current order visibility and status transitions, payment/refund history, Fresh Pick stock/reservations, staff login and passkeys, catalogue/pricing/photos, reports, and storefront checkout using safe read-only checks or isolated synthetic tests. Never seed or synchronize DEV data into Production.

### Release gate and smallest next action

Before any future Production change, the release owner must verify a current Production backup/PITR capability and retention, determine whether restore can target an isolated environment, and document the transaction-loss window and handling of writes after the recovery point. In parallel, preserve an identified READY application artifact; do not assume rebuild equivalence while deployed source remains unknown. Continue to exclude Fresh Pick Order Cutoff, deferred Ready-for-Collection changes, and Staff Administration V1. Existing release gates for refund callers, current-row compatibility, exact migration hash, schema drift, stability, and migration monitoring remain in force.

**One smallest next action:** In the Supabase Production project dashboard, read and record the current Database Backups/PITR status, retention, latest recoverable timestamp, and whether a restore can be created in an isolated target. If those controls are unavailable on the current plan, record that exact limitation for the owner; do not change plan/configuration or initiate a restore as part of this check.

## WOS cross-device synchronization investigation — 21 October 2026

- **Status: targeted Collection-board correction prepared for DEV review; overall WOS synchronization is not yet verified or complete.** Production remains unchanged at `a63fd0042c0826621f80dd85446e32d49d92b958`, Vercel `dpl_CuTVHzrrSbxfeGwQ9g9Brx7YEKUn`, and migration ledger 166.
- Confirmed Collection root cause: `CollectionLiveBoard` created a Supabase channel and subscribed without registering any event handlers. The board therefore depended on its 30-second fallback poll for cross-device order changes. The unused per-order path also was not wired to events.
- Narrow DEV change: register `orders` INSERT/UPDATE handlers for guest-order rows, coalesce events before reloading the authorized tab/date query, reconcile on initial/reconnected subscription and browser foreground/focus/online, retain the 30-second fallback, keep the last successful list on refresh failure, and remove timers/listeners/channel on cleanup. No database migration or permission change.
- Other inspected order boards (Bakery, Operations, Home and Whole Cake Calendar) already register order events plus fallback polling. Their end-to-end delivery and visual latency were not measured during this investigation. Supabase DEV/Production publication inspection showed `orders`, `order_timeline_events`, and `staff_notification_events`; `extra_stock` is not published. Home has an `extra_stock` listener, but it cannot receive Postgres Changes while that table is outside the publication. The EXTRA board currently uses a 30-second refresh. Do not add `extra_stock` to Realtime without a separate column/RLS privacy review because its rows include internal fields.
- Latency remains **NOT VERIFIED** by a two-active-session measurement. Source proves the Collection no-handler defect, but there is no client timestamp evidence to attribute the original system-wide 10–30 second symptom among database commit, event delivery, query refresh and rendering. Safe DEV two-session verification is still required before declaring the WOS issue resolved.

### Issue #8 — Phase 1 add-on catalogue audit — 1 November 2026

**Result: AUDIT COMPLETE / IMPLEMENTATION HOLD.** This was a read-only review. No application source, database rows/schema, migration history, Vercel settings, deployments, or aliases were changed.

**Verified source/environment identities**

- Issue branch at audit start: `codex/issue-8-addons-planning-20261101`, commit `474c83caf6cfcaead0835fc7ee60f564fa8e8a4d`, tree `2910e9bf13488504c0506145f22391e01afd9013`, parent Production source `d98afb9733ed8285e71ba7e4a7e7a3e1116980c7`. GitHub comparison showed one commit ahead, zero behind, with only this status document changed.
- Production Supabase `cuowwlduhxxifmpvpltt`: project `project-flour-prod`, ACTIVE_HEALTHY, PostgreSQL 17.6.1.166. Read-only catalog inspection only.
- DEV Supabase `tzwtpxdcggesgjaqxkqr`: project `project-flour-dev`, ACTIVE_HEALTHY, PostgreSQL 17.6.1.155. Read-only catalog inspection only.
- Existing Production deployment and aliases remain as previously recorded; this audit created no Preview and did not switch `dev.whitebird.asia`.

**Schema, access and historical-order findings**

- Both databases contain `complimentary_item_types`, `collection_complimentary_items`, `paid_addon_types`, `order_complimentary_items`, and `order_paid_addons`, with matching inspected column contracts. Complimentary definitions currently have only code/name; the collection join already stores availability, default inclusion, default quantity, sort order and customer-selectability. Paid definitions already store active state, price, shorthand, max quantity and sort order, but paid applicability is currently global.
- RLS is enabled. Complimentary definitions and their collection join grant authenticated users an unrestricted all-operation policy (`USING true`, `WITH CHECK true`); this is not Owner-only. Paid definitions allow authenticated SELECT and have no authenticated write policy. Customer option reads use the SECURITY DEFINER `storefront_customer_preorder_options(uuid)`, executable by anon/authenticated, rather than direct anon table reads. Existing paid-order snapshot tables have broad authenticated policies; they must remain separate from catalogue-management authorization.
- A safe Owner-only management boundary is feasible: retain authenticated read behavior; grant catalogue mutations only to a current `auth.uid()` staff profile with `is_active=true`, `archived_at IS NULL`, and role code `owner`. The application’s existing `canManageLibrary` also permits Managers, so the new actions must use a dedicated Owner check. The self-profile RLS policy and roles read policy provide a non-recursive identity lookup; this still needs an isolated role test.
- Historical snapshots are structurally protected from catalogue renames/deactivation: complimentary order rows store their own name, quantity and sort order, and their nullable type FK uses ON DELETE SET NULL. Paid order rows store code, name, unit_price, financial shorthand and quantity with a nullable type FK. `order_paid_addons_subtotal(uuid)` sums the order-line snapshot `unit_price * quantity`; the sync helper inserts live name/price only for new lines and retains existing line snapshots when editing. No order rows were read or changed.

**Checkout and operational call graph reviewed**

- Whole-cake Preorder loads options through the public option RPC, posts selected IDs/codes from the checkout action, then calls `submit_guest_preorder` or the catalogue-voucher wrapper. The database submission and paid-addon helper establish server-authoritative price snapshots.
- Fresh Pick loads the same option RPC after resolving the pickup-date collection, then calls `submit_guest_extra_order`. The current 17-argument RPC receives the pickup date, complimentary and paid selections, and routes paid lines through the same snapshot helper.
- Staff order creation delegates through `create_staff_guest_preorder_with_source_detail` to the private preorder creator. Existing order editing uses `save_guest_order_workspace_atomic` and `sync_guest_order_paid_addons`; retained paid lines preserve their snapshot.
- Payment totals and settlement read `order_paid_addons` snapshots; no catalogue price is used to reprice an existing order.

**Material compatibility risks found — stop before implementation**

1. Current standard Preorder and Fresh Pick database submit functions still whitelist only the existing complimentary codes (`birthday_topper`, `candle`, `knife`) and paid card codes (`birthday_card`, `wishing_card`). Thus a new UI or schema alone would either reject new items or fail to enforce new date/selectability rules. The option RPC also accepts only a collection ID, not pickup date. Dynamic, date-aware server validation must be added to the option path and every submission path; paid price must continue to come from the live catalog only for new order lines.
2. Production has a legacy SECURITY DEFINER Fresh Pick overload, `submit_guest_extra_order(text,text,text,date,time,text,uuid,boolean,boolean,jsonb)`, executable by anon/authenticated. It independently inserts complimentary snapshot lines and contains the same hard-coded codes; it has no seasonal-date contract. This overload is absent from DEV. The extended 17-argument overload exists in both databases and its inspected function-body fingerprint matches, as do the inspected shared option, preorder, paid-sync, staff-create and workspace-save functions. A future migration must preserve or safely wrap the legacy signature and test it; a DEV-only test of the extended overload is not sufficient for Production compatibility.
3. The existing broad authenticated write policies on complimentary catalogue tables must be narrowed for Owner-only management while preserving reads, collection seeding, guest checkout SECURITY DEFINER behavior, and all order-snapshot writes. This is a security-sensitive policy change requiring non-bypass role tests.
4. The Issue #8 planning record requires DEV parity to be stabilized before an Issue #8 Preview. The latest recorded DEV parity state remains HOLD before the `dev.whitebird.asia` switch. No Issue #8 Preview was created. This branch therefore cannot yet receive actual Preview acceptance.

**Minimum additive design for a reviewed implementation**

- Add a hard-gate active flag to complimentary item definitions; paid definitions already have `is_active`.
- Add nullable date bounds and an ordered-range constraint to the existing complimentary collection join.
- Add a separate collection-to-paid-add-on join (availability, customer-selectability, per-collection order, optional inclusive date bounds), backfilled to preserve existing globally offered paid cards for existing collections; seed future collections without changing order snapshots.
- Use the customer’s stored pickup/delivery DATE directly for bounds (Asia/Kuching business date); no timestamp conversion is needed after the date is chosen. Treat inactive as unavailable regardless of dates; when active, enforce inclusive date bounds. Empty bounds remain open.
- Keep existing order snapshot tables, totals and grants for guest checkout intact. Add Owner-scoped catalogue write policies/actions only. No schema or function migration has been authored or applied.

**Next gate:** stabilize DEV parity and verify the isolated Preview’s DEV-only bindings/rollback path; then implement the date-aware catalogue and Owner-only manager on this separate branch, including the Production-only legacy Fresh Pick overload in an isolated PostgreSQL compatibility rehearsal. Test stale-cart rejection, all four date boundaries, cross-collection isolation, inactive/expired items, snapshot retention, payment totals, and both customer checkout flows before creating a DEV Preview. No Production release is authorized.



### Issue #8 Phase 1B — Compatibility and safety rehearsal — 1 November 2026

**Result: GO for isolated DEV implementation planning and rehearsal; HOLD for any Production release.** This records bounded read-only hosted checks and disposable local PostgreSQL/PostgREST rehearsals. No hosted database, application source, migration, deployment, environment variable, or domain alias was changed.

**Compatibility matrix**

| Area | Result | Evidence and limit |
|---|---|---|
| Catalog and order snapshot schema | **PASS — read-only inventory** | Both DBs have the five audited catalog/snapshot tables with matching inspected columns. Complimentary definitions lack active/date fields; comp collection joins lack dates; paid definitions have active/price but are global. Collection counts differ (Production 3; DEV 6). |
| Owner-only authorization | **PASS — isolated prototype only** | Disposable PG 17.11 with non-bypass authenticated role: active Owner allowed; Manager, ordinary staff, non-staff, inactive Owner, archived Owner denied. Authenticated reads preserved; service-role bypass and anonymous RPC access checked. Predicate used verified `staff_profiles.auth_user_id=auth.uid()`, active and unarchived Owner. This was not an exact full-schema policy restore. |
| Seasonal, collection, inactive, quantity and price rules | **PASS — isolated prototype** | Inclusive date bounds and out-of-window cases, collection isolation, inactive rejection, paid quantity cap, server catalog price over client-supplied price, and preservation of order snapshot name/price/total after catalog edits/deactivation passed with synthetic rows. |
| Checkout option RPC compatibility | **PASS — local PostgREST routing probe** | Existing one-argument `storefront_customer_preorder_options(uuid)` is SECURITY DEFINER and executable by anon/authenticated. New date-aware overload must require the date (no default); retain the one-argument RPC for old-app compatibility. Hosted PostgREST routing was not independently identified. |
| Fresh Pick overloads | **INCOMPLETE — full-body compatibility** | Production has legacy 10-argument and extended 16-argument `submit_guest_extra_order`; DEV has only the 16-argument overload. Production overloads are postgres-owned SECURITY DEFINER and executable by anon/authenticated/service_role. Current extended client sends 15 args for non-delivery and 16 for delivery. |
| Production trigger/checkout execution | **INCOMPLETE** | Definitions and metadata were read, but exact trigger dependencies and checkout transaction bodies were not reconstructed and run end-to-end. No claim of complete Production-equivalent rehearsal. |
| Historical orders | **PASS — structural evidence only** | Order snapshot columns and subtotal helper establish that historical line values drive totals. No customer order rows were read or modified. Mutation behavior still needs implementation tests. |
| Hosted changes | **PASS — none** | No SQL, migration, ledger, deployment, environment, alias, order, or stock changes occurred. |

**Overload experiment and design constraint**

- Disposable PostgREST 16.4 used marker functions with exact Production 10/16 argument names, types, and defaults. Existing defaults produced HTTP 300 `PGRST203` ambiguity for a 10-key named call; a 16-key call resolved to the extended marker. This is a local routing result, not proof of a Production customer request failure or hosted PostgREST version.
- PostgreSQL rejected `CREATE OR REPLACE` when removing existing defaults. In a disposable transaction, dropping/recreating only the extended 16-argument function without CASCADE, restoring owner, SECURITY DEFINER, `search_path=public`, ACLs and signature, and requiring arguments 8–15 while retaining only arg16's default, preserved the 10-arg overload and resolved local PostgREST routing for 10-key legacy, 15-key current pickup/dine-in, and 16-key delivery calls. A forced transaction failure restored the prior definition. This is a candidate design only; exact audited function body/dependencies still need full isolated execution.
- Before implementation, test real bodies and transactions for both overloads under anon/authenticated roles. Do not drop with CASCADE or change the legacy signature.

**Minimum additive migration boundaries for DEV implementation**

1. Add `complimentary_item_types.is_active BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE`; add nullable inclusive `available_from`/`available_until` bounds and ordered-range constraints to complimentary collection mappings.
2. Add a separate collection-to-paid-add-on mapping for availability, customer selection, order and inclusive date bounds. Backfill existing collections with currently offered active `birthday_card` and `wishing_card`, dates open, preserving existing storefront behavior and comp mappings.
3. Preserve authenticated catalog reads and guest SECURITY DEFINER checkout access. Replace broad authenticated catalog writes with a dedicated active, unarchived Owner check; keep order snapshot policies separate. The broader Owner+Manager library permission is not sufficient.
4. Preserve `storefront_customer_preorder_options(uuid)`; add a required-date overload with no default, retaining the old one-argument behavior.
5. Validate collection/date, active state, customer-selectability, quantity and authoritative catalog price at every customer and staff server/RPC path, including standard preorder, both Production Fresh Pick overloads, staff create/edit and stale-cart submission. Snapshot current name/price only for new order lines; never reprice existing snapshots/totals.
6. Treat extended Fresh Pick overload default/routing adjustment as a separately reviewed part of the migration and preserve owner, security mode, search path, grants, idempotency and transaction behavior.

No migration was authored or applied in this phase.

**Rehearsal, migration sequence and recovery**

- Before applying anything to DEV, recreate exact relevant Production table/function/trigger definitions, ACLs, roles, dependencies and ledger metadata in disposable PostgreSQL 17. The simplified prototype does not meet that bar. Apply each new migration separately; verify definitions, ACLs, rollback and behavior after each. Record checksum/canonical version and do not bulk-sync or repair unrelated migration history.
- Keep the existing READY application deployment as rollback. If application rollback is needed, restore the prior compatible app while retaining additive DB schema/functions and use forward recovery unless a DB rollback is separately rehearsed. Do not delete catalog mappings or snapshots or restore an older database over live orders.
- Before a DEV Preview, verify protected DEV runtime credentials without exposing values and preserve the current alias target. No alias change is part of this phase.

**Regression evidence and decision**

- Existing Issue #7 parity CI `41273824740`: **PASS** at `07e56bd651914f1dbe5968cf82508a59dd15fca4`. Deployed parity app source `eaff4ce246c8655cb1fae30499058d746839074a` differs only by status documentation.
- No Issue #8 application changes exist, so TypeScript/lint/build/checkout regressions were not run. The database prototypes are targeted experiments, not the Issue #8 regression suite.
- **GO** to begin isolated DEV implementation, with the overload routing finding treated as a required design/test constraint. **HOLD** for a DEV migration until exact function bodies/triggers and checkout behavior pass production-equivalent rehearsal. **HOLD** for Production pending a separate authorization and release-specific validation. The explicit DEV alias is reconciled; stale auto-alias configuration and protected service-role runtime validity remain tracked limitations.

### Issue #8 Phase 2A — Exact Production-contract rehearsal — 1 November 2026

**Result: GO to isolated Phase 2B implementation with the overload compatibility migration included; HOLD for applying any migration to DEV/Production or deploying.** This was local-only. No hosted DB, app, Vercel configuration, alias, order, stock, or migration history changed.

- Production Supabase `cuowwlduhxxifmpvpltt`: active PostgreSQL 17.6.1.166. Read-only ledger check: 179 rows, 179 unique versions, max `20261031231126`; no `20261101194235` entry. DEV Supabase `tzwtpxdcggesgjaqxkqr` remained untouched. No Issue #8 hosted deployment or alias was created.

**Production-equivalent local reconstruction**

- Disposable macOS PostgreSQL 17.11 cluster, local-only database `prodcompat`; PostgREST 16.4 was bound only to localhost for request-shape resolution. All fixtures were synthetic and each workflow test ran in a transaction ended by `ROLLBACK`.
- The reconstructed public schema came from read-only Production catalogs: 67 public tables/704 columns; 408 regular constraints plus the captured constraint-trigger definitions; 235 indexes; 16 enums; 155 RLS policies; 79 actual triggers; function ownership/security/search-path/ACL metadata; and the actual transitive function-body set used by the tested paths. No customer rows were copied. No trigger was replaced with a no-op.

The Vault boundary had an empty local-only `vault.decrypted_secrets` relation to compile a dependency; no secret was copied or read. Thus this is an exact relevant public-schema/function-contract rehearsal, not a byte-for-byte Supabase platform image.

**Observed Fresh Pick overload defect and proposed correction**

- Production signatures are the legacy 10-argument `submit_guest_extra_order(text,text,text,date,time,text,uuid,boolean,boolean,jsonb)` with 3 defaults and the extended 16-argument overload with 9 defaults. Both are postgres-owned `SECURITY DEFINER`, `search_path=public`, and execute-granted to `anon`, `authenticated`, and `service_role`.
- Local PostgREST 16.4 reproduced HTTP 300 `PGRST203` for the legacy 10-key named-argument request because the extended overload also defaulted those same argument names. The current 15-key Pickup/Dine-in and 16-key Delivery payloads resolved to the extended body. This proves the local routing ambiguity; Production’s PostgREST version and live HTTP behavior were not independently probed.
- Proposed migration `supabase/migrations/20261101194235_issue8_fresh_pick_overload_compatibility.sql`, SHA-256 `6dda6c7ed675f4dbbb184166601fdb5189dfe60eb7c75c9d0fdaf0d69b547b8a`. It drops only the exact extended signature without `CASCADE`, recreates the exact Production function body, makes arguments 8–15 required, preserves the final argument default, restores postgres ownership, `SECURITY DEFINER`, `search_path=public`, the exact execute grants and Production function comment. It leaves the legacy overload and signature unchanged. This is a proposed candidate migration; no hosted copy was applied.
- Rehearsal used `psql -1` (one transaction). A forced `1/0` failure after DDL rolled back to the baseline extended overload with 9 defaults. Successful application produced 1 default on the extended overload and retained 3 defaults on legacy, with owner/security/search-path/ACL matching the captured Production metadata. PostgREST then resolved 10-, 15-, and 16-key calls to the intended real bodies; deliberately nonexistent synthetic stock was rejected as `P0001 Extra is not available`, and the database retained zero order rows after the HTTP probes.

**Actual function-body tests after applying the proposed migration locally**

- **PASS** legacy 10-argument body: synthetic confirmed stock became sold, order and order-item snapshot created, and the stock/order link was correct; the outer transaction rolled back after assertions.
- **PASS** extended 15-argument Pickup body and 16-argument body: actual captured function bodies executed with synthetic stock. Catalog-derived paid add-on prices and message snapshot were preserved. The 16-argument case exercised the final optional argument with Pickup; delivery-specific fulfillment/fee acknowledgment was not end-to-end exercised.
- **PASS** invalid legacy complimentary selection failed after the function’s order/stock steps; exception rollback left no new order and did not sell the fixture stock.
- **PASS** current one-argument `storefront_customer_preorder_options(uuid)` and actual standard `submit_guest_preorder(...)` body with a synthetic active collection, cake/size, complimentary item, and paid card. Catalog price was used. Editing both catalog item names and the paid price afterward did not change either order snapshot.
- **PASS** actual `create_staff_guest_preorder_with_source_detail(...)`, its exact Production `staff_preorder_private.create_waiting_list_preorder_with_source_detail(...)` dependency, and `save_guest_order_workspace_atomic(...)` ran with a synthetic authenticated active Owner session. Repeating the same edit left exactly one paid-add-on line and one message slot.
- **PASS — proposed policy pattern only** against the reconstructed tables: a `SECURITY DEFINER`, fixed-empty-search-path helper checking `staff_profiles.auth_user_id=auth.uid()`, active/unarchived profile and `roles.code='owner'` allowed Owner writes and denied Manager, ordinary staff, non-staff, and anonymous writes; `service_role` retained its platform bypass. The helper/policies were created only inside a local transaction and rolled back. Current Production still has broad authenticated `USING(true)` write policies on complimentary definitions and collection mappings; replacing those safely is required before an Owner-only manager can be released. `paid_addon_types` currently has an authenticated read policy and no authenticated write policy.

**Existing regression checks on the unchanged Production application source**

- `npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-audit --no-fund`: PASS.
- `npx tsc --noEmit`: PASS.
- `npm run test:release-regressions`: **57 PASS, 0 FAIL, 1 BLOCKED**. The blocked embedded disposable-PG assignment test hit `shmget: Operation not permitted` while initializing its own cluster. The isolated PostgreSQL 17.11 tests above are separate evidence and do not convert that bundled test to PASS.
- `npm run lint`: **FAIL on the existing baseline**, 77 errors/26 warnings across unrelated files (including existing `require()` and React effect diagnostics); no application file changed in this phase.
- `npm run build`: **INCOMPLETE/FAIL in this environment**, Next 16.2.12 could not fetch Google Fonts (`Newsreader`, `Outfit`) because network access to fonts.googleapis.com failed. No application change caused this.
- No Issue #8 UI, server action, catalogue schema, or hosted regression has been implemented. No Issue #8 migration is applied to DEV or Production.

**Phase 2B boundaries and recovery**

1. Keep the overload correction as a separately reviewed first migration; preserve the legacy function and never use `CASCADE`. Apply it transactionally through the approved migration mechanism only after separate authorization and fresh identity checks.
2. Add `complimentary_item_types.is_active` and inclusive date bounds to complimentary collection mappings. Add a separate collection-paid-addon mapping with availability/selectability/date bounds; backfill existing collection/card availability to match current behavior before switching reads.
3. Add a required-date options overload while retaining the one-argument RPC. Add server-side eligibility/quantity/authoritative-price checks to standard preorder, both Fresh Pick overload paths, staff create/edit, and stale-cart submission. Snapshot current display name and price only for new lines; preserve order snapshot tables and totals.
4. Replace broad complimentary authenticated write policies with active, unarchived Owner-only writes; retain existing authenticated read behavior and anon checkout RPC grants. Do not use the Owner+Manager Library capability for this narrower boundary.
5. Continue on the implementation branch only. Before any DEV migration, rehearse every migration individually with current exact function bodies/triggers, run checkout/date/collection/Owner-RLS/snapshot regressions, and preserve the existing DEV deployment as rollback. Roll back application code independently; do not restore old customer data or remove additive schema blindly.

**Decision:** **GO** to isolated Phase 2B implementation and test with the overload correction and Owner-only policy behavior as required gates. **HOLD** for DEV migration/deployment until the complete feature and all relevant function bodies are updated and rehearsed. **HOLD** for Production; this work made no Production changes and grants no release authorization.

### Issue #8 Phase 2C — Production-derived PostgreSQL 17 rehearsal — 1 November 2026

**Decision: HOLD for any hosted DEV migration or Preview verification.** The isolated rehearsal reconstructed the relevant Production database contracts and applied both candidate migrations in order, but actual legacy Fresh Pick execution exposed a blocking incompatibility in the preserved 10-argument function. The remaining Fresh Pick 15/16-argument cases were not run after that failure. No hosted database, source application, deployment, alias, customer record, or migration history was changed.

**Preserved source and artifacts**

- Tested source: codex/issue-8-addons-implementation-20261101, commit ac5d13c3b79ff0b39daf740bfab0cb0e74725a64, tree 6ebca46ed2b70c36db445120fdaa86f12fda4cca; branch was clean and matched origin/codex/issue-8-addons-implementation-20261101 before this documentation-only update. The two migrations and application source were not edited.

- Migration checksums remain:
  - 20261101194235_issue8_fresh_pick_overload_compatibility.sql: 6dda6c7ed675f4dbbb184166601fdb5189dfe60eb7c75c9d0fdaf0d69b547b8a.
  - 20261101200000_issue8_addon_catalogue_schema_and_owner_management.sql: 62a50a5f14bc5a31c5153eda6f0a8ccc7fa992c74f73664663b21a5e65bae476.

**Reconstructed database and migration evidence**

- Read-only Production catalog/function/policy/grant captures were reconstructed in a disposable PostgreSQL 17.11 cluster. The relevant dependency closure contained 42 public tables, 106 public functions plus 3 staff_preorder_private functions, 51 triggers, 98 policies, captured ownership/security/search-path/grants, Production default privileges, required extensions, enums, indexes/constraints, and the 179 Production migration version/name rows. No customer or staff-authentication data was copied; Auth was represented with a local auth.users(id) shim and synthetic identities. This is a relevant-schema rehearsal, not a byte-for-byte Supabase platform image.
- Migrations were applied individually in the specified order, each in an explicit local transaction. The rehearsal ledger reached exactly 181 unique versions and recorded canonical versions 20261101194235 and 20261101200000, each once, with its source SQL in the statements array. The Production baseline had neither version.
- Forced failures after each migration and its ledger insert rolled back cleanly: migration 1 restored the original extended-function default-argument count and left no version; migration 2 left no new table or version. Both rollback checks passed. Temporary PostgreSQL cluster cleanup was verified after each failed rehearsal and after the final run.
- The migration-created Owner save RPC was callable by an active synthetic Owner and denied Manager, ordinary staff, non-staff and anonymous callers; it was not executable by service_role. The trusted service-role table privilege remained present. The date-aware customer options overload exposed inclusive start/end dates, excluded dates outside the window, maintained collection isolation, and the legacy one-argument options RPC retained nonseasonal items. These checks passed.
- An actual anonymous submit_guest_preorder call created synthetic order lines; the database used the catalogue price (19.99) rather than a supplied client value (0.01) and wrote current complimentary/paid snapshots. Stale-date, inactive-item and non-selectable-item submissions were rejected with no partial order. Owner catalogue edits preserved prior order item names, prices, complimentary snapshots and order_amount_due. The actual staff create/edit RPCs ran twice without duplicate paid-add-on rows; the staff order total remained consistent. These checks passed.
- A quantity-two standard Preorder attempt was rejected by the captured Production function's existing rule, “Paid add-on quantity for this order must be 1”, before the new catalogue maximum-quantity trigger could be evaluated. Rejection/rollback passed, but configurable quantities greater than one are not established for standard Preorder. The checkout option engine's existing customer quantity limit remains a separate restriction.
- Blocking failure — legacy Fresh Pick 10-argument RPC: calling the actual preserved legacy function body failed at the deferred enforce_order_size_availability_final constraint with “Fresh Pick requires legitimate stock linked to this order”. The body marks the synthetic stock sold and inserts orders.extra_stock_id, but unlike the current extended body it does not set the required extra_stock.order_id link. The captured Production constraint requires the direct link and matching physical stock. Migration 1 changes only the extended overload and leaves the legacy function body untouched; migration 2 does not repair it. The function call rolled back. Because the first Fresh Pick case failed, the current 15-argument Pickup and 16-argument Delivery bodies, Fresh Pick custom add-on behavior, and their compatibility remain INCOMPLETE. Do not attribute this failure to the new catalogue schema or hide the deferred guard.
- The migration SQL explicitly preserves the legacy signature, recreates the extended function with its intended defaults/owner/security/grants, retains the UUID-only options RPC, and adds the date-aware overload. The final post-migration signature/owner/grant comparison was not reached after the legacy Fresh Pick call failed; these postflight checks remain INCOMPLETE. No unrelated Production ACL comparison is claimed.

**Application verification**

- npx tsc --noEmit: PASS.
- Changed TypeScript/TSX/MJS scoped ESLint: PASS.
- scripts/test-customer-preorder-options.ts: PASS.
- scripts/test-guest-extra-paid-addons.ts: PASS.
- npm run test:release-regressions: 57 PASS, 0 FAIL, 1 BLOCKED in the restricted invocation because its disposable PostgreSQL child could not create shared memory (shmget: Operation not permitted). The exact existing scripts/test-extra-assignment-rpc-postgres.sh was then rerun with the installed PostgreSQL 17.11 tools outside that restriction and passed, including the two-session competing-assignment assertions and cleanup. This resolves that one blocked test's evidence; it does not resolve the Issue #8 legacy Fresh Pick failure.
- Exact-candidate npm run build: INCOMPLETE/FAIL in this network environment. Next.js 16.2.12 could not fetch Google Fonts Newsreader and Outfit from fonts.googleapis.com. No source workaround was made. The previously recorded full-repository lint baseline remains separate; this turn's changed-file lint passed.

**Compatibility limits and next gate**

- This rehearsal used actual relevant Production definitions and real bodies, not no-op trigger placeholders. It did not copy Production business rows or recreate every Supabase-managed service. PostgreSQL was local 17.11 versus Production 17.6.1.166.
- Before any DEV migration, the 10-argument Fresh Pick path must be reconciled with the required stock-order linkage without weakening the availability/assignment guards. Any correction must be separately reviewed and retested against the preserved current and legacy RPC signatures. Then run the 15-argument Pickup and 16-argument Delivery calls, Fresh Pick seasonal/active/non-selectable rejection and rollback, and the direct paid-line quantity guard. Do not modify historical migration history or apply either migration to hosted DEV as part of this status update.
- A future supported sequence remains overload compatibility migration first, then catalogue/schema migration, each with a preflight, exact checksum check, atomic application and postflight. Application rollback does not automatically reverse schema changes; preserve the existing application rollback independently and use forward database recovery unless a separately rehearsed SQL rollback is approved.

**Result:** Source is preserved. The migration syntax/transaction rehearsal and several actual checkout, authorization, seasonal, snapshot and staff-edit checks passed locally. The legacy Fresh Pick failure is a specific compatibility blocker, and the exact candidate build is network-limited. HOLD for hosted DEV migration and Preview verification.

### Issue #8 Phase 2D — legacy Fresh Pick stock-order linkage — 1 November 2026

**Decision: GO to a separately authorized DEV migration/verification stage; HOLD for Production.** The legacy RPC failure is reproduced against the captured Production baseline, and a narrowly scoped third migration fixes its missing reverse stock link. The three-migration rehearsal and existing Fresh Pick assignment/readiness regressions pass. No hosted DEV/Production database, order, deployment, environment variable, or alias was changed.

**Source and evidence identities**

- Branch: `codex/issue-8-addons-implementation-20261101`.
- Starting documentation commit: `08372fd9e86e4cb8bc5307910ee655b5a7bf4c05`, tree `9378ac05a2231e606033b71b3f31ef0ec0a7c712`.
- Application source under test remains `ac5d13c3b79ff0b39daf740bfab0cb0e74725a64`, tree `6ebca46ed2b70c36db445120fdaa86f12fda4cca`. The Phase 2D diff contains no application, TypeScript, JavaScript, checkout, pricing, payment, allocation, or readiness source changes.
- Production recovery anchor remains `d98afb9733ed8285e71ba7e4a7e7a3e1116980c7`; no Production mutation was performed.
- Read-only relevant-schema/function capture: `/private/tmp/issue8-production-relevant-schema.sql`, SHA-256 `1995f4249786e322354bfe54fd57df781879af69d9d5babe9fa5924e94844f0f`. It captured the relevant Production dependency set (42 public tables, 106 public functions plus 3 private-schema functions, 51 triggers, 98 policies, grants/default privileges, enums, constraints, indexes, and 179 migration version/name rows), without customer or staff records. This is a relevant schema reconstruction, not a full Supabase platform image. The rehearsal ran on local PostgreSQL 17.11; Production’s captured server version was 17.6.1.166.

**Diagnosis — baseline versus Issue #8**

- The captured legacy 10-argument `public.submit_guest_extra_order` is a real `SECURITY DEFINER` function owned by `postgres`, with `search_path=public` and existing EXECUTE grants to `anon`, `authenticated`, and `service_role`. The current extended overload is 16 arguments. The deployed application source calls the extended RPC with named parameters from `src/workspaces/storefront/extra/actions.ts`; no deployed application call to the legacy 10-argument form was found in the current or Production-recovery application source. The repository does not establish whether an external integration calls it. A bounded Production PostgREST log look at the preceding 24-hour window did not expose usable RPC paths/argument arity, so external use is **UNKNOWN**, not ruled out.
- The deferred Production `orders_size_availability_final` / `order_items_stock_linkage_final` trigger calls `enforce_order_size_availability_final`. For Fresh Pick orders it requires the referenced physical row’s `extra_stock.order_id` to equal the order ID, the stock to be confirmed and uncut, and linked physical quantities to cover the cake lines. It validates linkage; it does not create the reverse link.
- The existing `extra_stock_event_on_order_link` trigger writes the sold/order-link audit event when `orders.extra_stock_id` is set. It does not set `extra_stock.order_id`. The other captured stock triggers likewise do not establish this reverse relationship.
- On the **untouched** captured Production baseline, a synthetic 10-argument call with the extended overload’s defaults still present is rejected as an ambiguous function call (`is not unique`) before either function body executes. Assertions confirmed no order, stock, or audit changes.
- After Issue #8 migration 1 removes defaults from the extended overload (and migration 2 makes its reviewed catalogue allowlist replacement), the same 10-argument request resolves to the legacy body. That body marks the stock sold and inserts `orders.extra_stock_id` but omits `extra_stock.order_id`; the unchanged deferred integrity trigger rejects commit with `Fresh Pick requires legitimate stock linked to this order`. Transaction rollback leaves no order or changed stock/audit state. The legacy function’s body outside migration 2’s specific complimentary allowlist replacement matched the captured Production body. This is a pre-existing mismatch between the legacy body and the current constraint; Issue #8 migration 1 exposes it by making the legacy overload unambiguous, but neither Issue #8 migration supplies the missing link.
- Therefore the observed linkage defect is not a new allocation-rule defect introduced by the catalogue schema. The baseline 10-argument API was already unusable due overload ambiguity; once made addressable, its preserved body was incompatible with the deferred linkage contract. The minimal correction is warranted to preserve the advertised legacy signature and its existing grants without weakening the constraint.

**Phase 2D correction**

- Added `supabase/migrations/20261101200100_issue8_legacy_fresh_pick_order_stock_link.sql`, SHA-256 `2bb8d8755b524d23e0ed0d7ae4712df93747764c8cba120d9bef3faa9ec79dbf`.
- The migration checks the reviewed 10-argument function identity, `postgres` ownership, SECURITY DEFINER status, `search_path=public`, and existing EXECUTE grants. It performs one exact, unique body-anchor replacement using `CREATE OR REPLACE`: after the order insert returns its ID, it links the already locked, already validated sold/confirmed/uncut stock row to that order and requires exactly one affected row. The existing row lock, eligibility checks, order/item snapshots, event trigger, deferred constraint, and transaction remain authoritative.
- The signature, defaults, owner, ACL, security mode, and search path are unchanged. The function still uses the same deferred trigger; no constraint, grant, trigger, allocation rule, payment/order behavior, or global privilege was weakened or replaced. No data backfill is included.
- Full future candidate sequence is: `20261101194235_issue8_fresh_pick_overload_compatibility.sql` (SHA-256 `6dda6c7ed675f4dbbb184166601fdb5189dfe60eb7c75c9d0fdaf0d69b547b8a`), then `20261101200000_issue8_addon_catalogue_schema_and_owner_management.sql` (SHA-256 `62a50a5f14bc5a31c5153eda6f0a8ccc7fa992c74f73664663b21a5e65bae476`), then `20261101200100_issue8_legacy_fresh_pick_order_stock_link.sql` (SHA-256 above). These migrations remain unapplied to hosted DEV and Production.

**PostgreSQL 17 rehearsal — final run PASS**

- Loaded actual captured Production definitions and dependencies, not placeholder trigger bodies, into a disposable PostgreSQL 17.11 database. Used synthetic identities and order/stock fixtures only. The local cluster’s temporary directory was removed; output ended `TEMP_CLUSTER_CLEANUP=PASS`.
- Before migrations: legacy positional call ambiguity reproduced; no partial order, stock mutation, or audit change (`UNTOUCHED_BASELINE_LEGACY_10_FAILURE_ROLLBACK=PASS`). After migrations 1 and 2, the legacy function fingerprint was normalized only for migration 2’s exact complimentary-catalogue patch, confirming no other body changes; the deferred-linkage failure reproduced with no partial writes (`ISSUE8_BASELINE_COMPARISON_LEGACY_FAILURE_UNCHANGED=PASS`).
- Migration 1 and 2 forced-failure rollback checks passed. Migration 3 forced failure rolled back both the `CREATE OR REPLACE` body change and its test ledger insert; the canonical version was absent after rollback (`MIGRATION_3_ATOMIC_ROLLBACK=PASS`). Applying all three in order recorded each canonical version once; the ledger uniqueness assertion passed. No unrelated migration ran.
- After migration 3, actual anonymous legacy 10-argument checkout passed. The result had matching `orders.extra_stock_id` and `extra_stock.order_id`, sold stock, and the expected sold/order-link audit metadata. Repeated use of that stock was rejected with no duplicate order/audit writes. Rejected stock was also denied with no order, stock, or audit mutation.
- Actual current 15-argument Pickup and 16-argument Delivery calls passed; physical rows were sold and linked. Delivery details and server-authoritative paid-addon snapshots were verified. The legacy and extended signatures, grants, ownership and SECURITY DEFINER properties remained intact; the legacy search path/defaults/ACL/signature metadata matched its pre-patch values.
- Existing Issue #8 tests in the same rehearsal passed: Owner-only catalogue RPC; Manager/staff/non-staff/anon/service-role denial boundaries; inclusive dates and collection isolation; Preorder options and authoritative pricing; stale-cart, inactive, non-selectable and quantity rejection; staff order create/edit/idempotency; and historical complimentary/paid snapshots and totals.
- Existing Fresh Pick regression checks passed: `scripts/test-extra-assignment-rpc-postgres.sh` on PostgreSQL 17.11 (`concurrent_competing_assignment=PASS`, `assignment_rpc_postgres=PASS`, isolated synthetic mode), `scripts/test-extra-assignment-eligibility.cjs`, `scripts/test-fresh-pick-assignment-readiness-contract.ts`, `scripts/test-extra-phase6-fresh-picks.ts`, `scripts/test-extra-fresh-picks-eligibility.ts`, `scripts/test-bakery-fresh-pick-allocation.ts`, `scripts/test-bakery-fresh-pick-readiness.ts`, and `scripts/test-fresh-pick-cart.ts` all passed. `scripts/test-fresh-pick-complimentary-regressions.ts` passed after using the local TypeScript transpiler/alias loader; no code was changed for the test runner.

**Build and lint**

- Exact candidate `npm run build`: **PASS** with Google Fonts reachable; Next.js 16.2.12 compiled, completed TypeScript, generated 47 pages and finalized optimization. The migration is database-only and does not change application code.
- `./node_modules/.bin/tsc --noEmit`: **PASS**.
- Scoped ESLint invocation reported existing errors in unchanged `scripts/test-extra-assignment-eligibility.cjs`, `src/workspaces/storefront/extra/GuestExtraCheckoutForm.tsx`, and `src/workspaces/storefront/extra/GuestExtraOrderForm.tsx`, plus existing warnings. Those files are identical to the tested application commit; Phase 2D adds no TS/JS files or new lint violations. No unrelated source was edited to hide this baseline.

**Recovery and next gate**

- The migration has no data backfill and changes only the legacy function body. Its forced-transaction-failure rehearsal proved atomic rollback. If applied in a future environment, application rollback is independent from SQL. Reverting the function body to the captured original would reintroduce the legacy failure; prefer retaining this compatible correction and using forward database recovery. No order or stock restoration is called for.
- No hosted DEV migration, Vercel deployment, alias switch, or Production operation occurred. Phase 2D correction and rehearsal are ready for a separately authorized hosted DEV migration/Preview verification stage. Production remains **HOLD** pending that DEV verification, separate review/authorization, and its own live release gates.

**Reviewed migration compatibility correction**

1. M1 remains unchanged and patches/replaces the required extended Fresh Pick overload without inventing a legacy RPC.
2. M2 now resolves required Preorder/extended functions safely and treats only the exact legacy 10-argument signature as optional. It rejects unexpected overload counts and validates the expected owner, security, search path, definition and grants before changing functions. On Production-style RLS it leaves the deferred validator unchanged. ## Issue #8 Phase 2G-R2 — DEV logical restore verification (2 November 2026)

**Result: GO to resume the previously authorized DEV-only migration sequence after its immediate preflight.** The existing DEV archive restored successfully into an isolated, compatible Supabase PostgreSQL environment and passed the relevant catalog, grant, ledger and data-presence comparisons. No hosted DEV migrations were applied during this recovery task. Production was not accessed.

### Preserved archive and local restore environment

- Rechecked the existing archive; SHA-256 remains `4c67bb6df3ad4d2fcbe7a06adb37f3781d7558fb197031b296942ec4d446d720`, size 4,950,659 bytes, mode 600. Its enclosing private directory is mode 700; the mode-600 local manifest and cleanup log are outside the repository. The archive and sensitive contents were not uploaded to GitHub.
- Local environment: Docker Desktop 29.8.2 on Apple Silicon; Supabase CLI 2.117.0; official image `public.ecr.aws/supabase/postgres:17.6.1.167`; database reports PostgreSQL 17.6. A unique, unlinked temporary Supabase project used port 55442; only its PostgreSQL service was started. Auth, Storage, API and other services were excluded.
- The nine DEV extensions were available and restored at matching versions: `btree_gist 1.7`, `citext 1.6`, `pg_cron 1.6.4`, `pg_net 0.20.4`, `pg_stat_statements 1.11`, `pgcrypto 1.3`, `plpgsql 1.0`, `supabase_vault 0.3.1`, and `uuid-ossp 1.1`.
- The archive restored into a fresh database using PostgreSQL 17.11 `pg_restore --single-transaction --exit-on-error`; final exit status was 0 and the restore log had no errors. An earlier atomic attempt as local `postgres` rolled back because that role could not `SET ROLE supabase_admin`; retrying as the local Supabase superuser completed. No partial state from the first attempt remained.
- Before restore, the disposable instance set `cron.database_name=flour_restore_r2` and `cron.launch_active_jobs=off`. This kept archived cron jobs from running. Those settings affected only the temporary local container.

### Restore comparisons

The restored catalog matched DEV in counts and fingerprints: 107 relations, 572 functions, 87 non-internal triggers, 557 constraints, 163 RLS policies, 370 indexes, one view, 26 enums and two sequences. Function body fingerprints matched by schema; trigger, constraint, policy, column, index, view, enum, sequence, default-ACL and migration-ledger fingerprints matched. Object owners and RLS flags matched. Normalized effective table, function and schema permissions matched after the supplemental schema-grant step below. Some raw ACL arrays serialize in a different order; their grantee/privilege/grant-option/grantor sets are identical.

Exact row counts matched for 21 relevant catalogue, order, payment, stock, snapshot, staff, Auth, Vault and migration tables. Key examples: 90 orders, 104 order items, 35 payments, 35 payment allocations, 68 Fresh Pick stock rows, 297 stock events, 33 order confirmation snapshots, 377 order timeline events, 7 Auth users, 82 Auth migration rows, 183 Supabase migration rows, and 2 encrypted Vault rows. The three Issue #8 migration versions remain absent. The existing migration-ledger discrepancy remains unchanged.

RPC and integrity metadata matched DEV: the current 16-argument Fresh Pick submission function remains SECURITY DEFINER under `postgres` with its existing grants; the DEV database still has no legacy 10-argument overload; the one-argument customer-options RPC remains present. Deferred stock/order constraint triggers and validated stock foreign keys match. A synthetic JWT claim returned its synthetic identity through `auth.uid()` in a transaction that was rolled back. No checkout or customer order was created.

The plain restore did not reproduce effective USAGE grants on the Supabase-managed `graphql` and `graphql_public` schemas. Those exact live grants were captured read-only and replayed **only in the disposable local database**; effective schema ACLs then matched. Include this verified local platform-schema grant step when using this archive as a comparison restore.

### Existing DEV data condition noted, not repaired

A read-only comparison found eight `orders.extra_stock_id` references whose stock row has a different/null `extra_stock.order_id`. All eight orders are cancelled; their referenced stock is unsold and has no direct order ID. The same counts and status/lifecycle aggregates appeared in the restored copy. No identifiers or customer fields were reported, and no rows were changed. This is a pre-existing historical DEV condition, not a restore difference. The Issue #8 migrations do not repair these references; review separately before any workflow that relies on the affected rows.

### Recovery limits and repeatable procedure

This is a **verified logical PostgreSQL database restore**, suitable as a recovery reference for the Issue #8 database objects and data. It is not a complete Supabase platform recovery package:

- The Auth database schema and seven user rows are present, and the synthetic `auth.uid()` check passed, but the GoTrue service was excluded; actual login and provider configuration were not restored or tested.
- The Vault extension/view and encrypted rows are present, but secret plaintext was not read and Vault key material is outside this database archive. Decryption is unverified.
- Storage database metadata may be in the dump; Storage object bytes, external credentials and other hosted service configuration are not.
- No checkout transaction was run. Checkout function identity/definition/grants and deferred integrity definitions were compared structurally; no customer or stock mutation was performed.

Repeatable isolated verification: provision a fresh, unlinked Supabase PostgreSQL 17 environment with the listed extension versions and required Supabase roles; keep cron execution disabled; create an empty restore database; set its `cron.database_name` to that database; restore with `pg_restore --single-transaction --exit-on-error` as `supabase_admin`; compare the relevant object/data fingerprints and effective grants; replay only the captured Supabase-managed GraphQL schema grants if the local image omits them. Never restore this archive into hosted DEV or Production as part of this procedure.

The temporary local Supabase project was stopped with `supabase stop --no-backup`; its container, volume, network, temporary workspace and port listener were verified absent. The original archive and manifest remain intact. The temporary DEV password file was removed and its absence verified. Docker Desktop was left running because unrelated pre-existing Docker projects were present; they were not touched.

### DEV alias and migration decision

Read-only Vercel checks reconfirmed that `dev.whitebird.asia` aliases READY deployment `dpl_FPF1M4NkkSXP6kKdJ4UpQxzWUbBR`. Its separate project-domain record still stores `gitBranch=codex/cake-size-availability-dev`. Neither alias nor branch association changed.

**GO for resuming the previously authorized DEV-only three-migration sequence**, subject to its existing immediate identity/checksum/schema/ledger preflight. This result does not authorize a Production action, DEV deployment, or alias change. No Issue #8 migration was applied in Phase 2G-R2.

The deferred delivery invariant retains its recorded DEV identity (SECURITY INVOKER, `search_path=public`, definition MD5 `3ddd53a414b040c3a048bd237c449d28`). The focused read-only checks did not establish a new drift condition.
