### Exact execution blocker

The available Supabase MCP migration tool accepts only `name`, `project_id`, and SQL; it does not accept a canonical migration version. The Supabase MCP implementation delegates to the Management API, which generates the migration timestamp at request time. That would create a new noncanonical ledger entry instead of the three approved file versions. Supabase's own MCP issue documents this limitation and identifies version mismatch as the result ([Supabase MCP issue #241](https://github.com/supabase/mcp/issues/241)). Using that tool would also violate the recorded rule not to create more generated-version discrepancies. The standard `supabase db push` route is not safe here because the branch records 19 local-only and 19 remote-only historical versions; broad synchronization/history repair is prohibited.

Supabase CLI 2.117.0 is installed, but its read-only project-list request failed with a transport error to `api.supabase.com` in this execution environment. No linked CLI migration or direct PostgreSQL migration was attempted. No alternative runner was available that both applies one selected file atomically and records its exact canonical filename version.

### Decision and continuation requirement

M1, M2, and M3 were **not run**; there are no post-migration assertions to report. Do not retry with MCP `apply_migration`, broad `db push`, direct ledger insertion, or manual history repair. Resume only when a supported, authenticated version-aware runner can apply each repository migration separately and record its exact version in the same transaction. Then repeat identity/schema/ledger preflight and verify each migration before proceeding to the next.


## Issue #8 Phase 2G-E — canonical migration runner qualification (2 November 2026)

**Scope:** runner qualification only. No hosted DEV migration, deployment, alias change, environment-variable edit, customer-data write, or Production access/change occurred.

**Verified source and migrations**

- Branch was codex/issue-8-addons-implementation-20261101, HEAD 5bec6057982006b22722a8a3e9b871eab1593b4d, tree a2cf62e9085dfd3743a141ae18bffb9c4f1de303 at the start of this review. The source branch did not change; this entry is documentation-only.
- The three approved migration files were checked against the staged branch files. Their SHA-256 values remain: M1 6dda6c7ed675f4dbbb184166601fdb5189dfe60eb7c75c9d0fdaf0d69b547b8a; M2 18788af12a64e10676b0e6352e13776b05cd7df7808f2688c7724044e08c25ba; M3 cb5272995f6704a552bf51f965e202d7f6b950d9f3cdbbf7a012fc0fc3018b27.
- The existing DEV logical backup remained at its private path outside GitHub, mode 600, 4,950,659 bytes, SHA-256 4c67bb6df3ad4d2fcbe7a06adb37f3781d7558fb197031b296942ec4d446d720. The previous restore-tested manifest remains available. No new backup was created.

**Supabase CLI assessment**

Installed CLI: 2.117.0. Its migration up help supports a target URL or linked project, but has no single-version selector; it applies pending files from the configured migrations directory. The isolated three-file workspace was rehearsed against the restored 183-entry DEV ledger and refused before SQL execution with LegacyMigrationMissingLocalError: Remote migration versions not found in local migrations directory. This is the recorded historical mismatch, not a migration SQL failure. The --include-all and db push options were rejected because they are not one-file controls and could select unrelated pending files.

**Alternative runner rehearsed**

A narrowly scoped PostgreSQL runner was tested on a fresh disposable restore of the existing backup using Supabase Postgres image 17.6.1.167 (server PostgreSQL 17.6) and host PostgreSQL client tools 17.11. The restore started with 183 ledger rows, maximum version 20261101100854, and none of the three target versions. Only temporary container-local role scaffolding needed by the restore was used. The container was removed after each rehearsal; cleanup verification passed.

The tested runner is a custom, one-file psql transaction, not supabase migration up: psql -X --single-transaction -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -f <generated-one-file-wrapper>. The wrapper sets transaction-local lock_timeout = 3s and statement_timeout = 5min, takes a transaction advisory lock, checks the target version is absent and required prior versions exist, includes the unchanged migration file, then inserts the exact version, filename-derived name, and parsed statement array into supabase_migrations.schema_migrations before commit. The parsed array used the Supabase CLI v2.117.0 legacy SQL splitter. The three migration files contain no non-transactional directive or transaction-control statement.

- Forced an error after M1 DDL and before its history insert: the transaction failed, M1 remained absent, and the 16-argument Fresh Pick function definition MD5 remained 9a62b3eacd01513fe6ca7ff138a72a08. Retrying M1 then succeeded.
- After M1: exactly one canonical 20261101194235 row with the matching name; ledger count 184; M2 and M3 absent.
- After separately running M2: exactly one canonical 20261101200000 row; ledger count 185; M3 absent.
- After separately running M3: exactly one canonical 20261101200100 row; ledger count 186.
- The fingerprint of the original 183 ledger rows remained 262cea08f6dfbb19418691d85f344219; no duplicate or generated versions appeared.
- supabase migration list --db-url exited 0 on the local restore and showed M1 as matching local and remote version 20261101194235. The other historical rows remained remote-only in the intentionally three-file workspace. This confirms canonical-version recognition; it does not clear the known full-history mismatch for broad future CLI operations.

PostgreSQL documents that psql --single-transaction wraps the script in one transaction and rolls it back when ON_ERROR_STOP is enabled and any command fails ([PostgreSQL 17 psql reference](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/app-psql.html)). Supabase documents that migration listing compares timestamps/versions, and that remote history is stored in supabase_migrations.schema_migrations ([migration list reference](https://supabase.com/docs/reference/cli/supabase-migration-list), [database migration guide](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/deployment/database-migrations)).

**Hosted DEV preflight and decision**

- Supabase project metadata and authenticated read-only SQL independently reconfirmed DEV tzwtpxdcggesgjaqxkqr, PostgreSQL 17.6, database postgres, role postgres, ledger count 183, maximum 20261101100854, and zero target migration versions. The current extended 16-argument Fresh Pick function remains postgres-owned, SECURITY DEFINER, search_path=public, MD5 9a62b3eacd01513fe6ca7ff138a72a08.
- The secure DEV password file previously used for backup is no longer present (PGPASSFILE_MISSING). No password was requested or exposed, and a direct TLS verify-full PostgreSQL identity check could not be run. The Supabase MCP read-only connection did verify project identity, but it is not the tested psql execution route.
- Therefore the runner qualification PASSES locally, but hosted DEV execution remains HOLD until an owner-provided mode-600 DEV password file and the current Dashboard connection parameters are available for a read-only TLS identity check. Do not guess the pooler host/user or fall back to MCP apply_migration, broad db push, history repair, or manual post-commit ledger changes.

For a later separately authorized DEV run: verify project tzwtpxdcggesgjaqxkqr and TLS identity; recheck each checksum and current ledger immediately before each file; use the exact one-file transaction wrapper above; verify each canonical version and dependent objects before continuing; stop on any failure. If commit acknowledgement is uncertain, inspect database state read-only before any retry. Preserve the logical backup; use forward recovery for committed additive changes rather than deleting ledger rows or undoing migration history.

**Result:** HOLD for hosted DEV migration execution pending a verified secure psql credential/connection. Production remains untouched.

## Issue #8 historical collection classification correction — 2026-11-02

- **Source:** branch `codex/issue-8-addons-implementation-20261101`; tested code commit `18168eaec6c847efee0c44f904ea6140f09e011f`; tree `d7fa4f591fc78402953d4df97d1b0225d68f7d4c`. Seven changed files versus prior status commit `dc8fcbb5994eb37a33fc1380713098d4676d00d8`: add-on manager, collection-date classifier and declaration, add-on query/types, owner save action, and focused regression script. No migration file, checkout availability rule, pricing rule, order, or database data changed.
- **Authoritative date model verified in source:** `20260816170000_catalogue_special_dates.sql` documents `collections.month` as the first day of the Singapore calendar month for monthly catalogues; monthly rows intentionally have null `start_date`/`end_date`. Special-occasion catalogues use an inclusive explicit start/end range. The existing monthly catalogue form and schema constraint confirm that model. Singapore and Kuching share the same UTC+8 business-day boundary; this classifier uses the app's Asia/Kuching date.
- **Correction:** monthly end dates are derived from the authoritative `month` value, including leap-year month ends. A collection is historical only after its calculated/explicit inclusive end date has passed. Active, draft, or archived labels do not override dates. Missing/invalid month, unknown purpose, incomplete/invalid special range, or invalid business date is classified ambiguous. Ambiguous collections are visible under a clear schedule-review warning, read-only in the UI, rejected by the server save action if submitted, and existing assignment rows are preserved.
- **Regression evidence — PASS:** [Actions run 41379061452](https://github.com/hyphenkk3/project-flour/actions/runs/41379061452) on the exact tested commit. Focused checks cover September 2026 classified historical in November, October/November month-end inclusivity, leap-year February, special-date boundary behavior, malformed/ambiguous schedules, rejected historical/ambiguous/unknown submitted IDs, and preservation of original historical and ambiguous assignment rows. TypeScript PASS; scoped ESLint PASS; existing release regressions **58 PASS, 0 FAIL, 0 BLOCKED**, including the disposable PostgreSQL 17 Fresh Pick assignment regression.
- **Preview — READY:** deployment `dpl_4yjYqEjiv8RMEA18H1TAACVvbYzL`, https://project-flour-bs6txjcml-hyphen-org.vercel.app, exact source SHA `18168eaec6c847efee0c44f904ea6140f09e011f`, tree above, Preview target, Vercel project `prj_3rmlAQzLSFOi15DEGczSuDYDq8xr` / `hyphen-org`, Node.js 24.x.
- **Environment:** branch Preview bindings remain scoped only to `codex/issue-8-addons-implementation-20261101`. The public Supabase URL resolves to DEV `tzwtpxdcggesgjaqxkqr`; the public key binding is present. The service-role binding is present as a protected Preview secret, with its value redacted and not independently verified. No Production binding was supplied as an override.
- **Boundaries:** `dev.whitebird.asia` remains assigned to `dpl_FPF1M4NkkSXP6kKdJ4UpQxzWUbBR`; its stored branch association was not changed. No hosted database writes/migrations, Production access, or Production changes occurred. Authenticated Owner visual review of the new manager remains the next acceptance step.


## Issue #8 — Owner New Order catalogue access (2026-11-02)

**Status: HOLD — source and DEV boundary reviewed; implementation and release verification remain incomplete.** This is an addendum to the existing Issue #8 branch history. No application or database changes were made for this request.

- **Verified source:** branch `codex/issue-8-addons-implementation-20261101`, HEAD `b83bd6c47e3cda2461ac3176f8a5e135ee412456`, tree `cdf6a86c66b2ef7d285476c0821fda4b976109f8`.
- **Manual form:** `src/app/(app)/owner/orders/new/page.tsx` is active-Owner-only. It loads Complimentary Items only through the current collection's mappings and loads Paid Add-ons through `listActivePaidAddonTypes()`, which filters `is_active = true`. This omits inactive items and items without a current collection mapping.
- **Date changes:** `StaffGuestOrderForm.tsx` changes pickup date by updating `pickupDate`; it does not reset complimentary or paid add-on state. Existing choices therefore remain selected on date changes.
- **Current server path:** the form action calls the Owner-guarded `createStaffGuestOrderAction`, then `createStaffGuestPreorderRecord`, which invokes `create_staff_guest_preorder_with_source_detail`. The current DEV RPC is SECURITY DEFINER, owned by `postgres`, and checks that `auth.uid()` maps to the active staff actor; its role allowlist includes Owner, Manager and Customer Operations. It has no Owner-only catalogue override.
- **DEV database evidence (read-only):** DEV project `tzwtpxdcggesgjaqxkqr`, PostgreSQL 17.6. The existing complimentary and paid line trigger validators are SECURITY DEFINER and reject newly selected items unless active and assigned/available for the order's computed collection/date. Paid lines are priced and snapshotted from catalogue rows by `_sync_order_paid_addons_from_payload(uuid,jsonb)`; the line trigger also enforces catalogue maximum quantity and prevents changing commercial snapshot fields on updates. Function-definition fingerprints observed: complimentary validator `ed2bdfff6784cc5791d2cb4a7dd53dd8`, paid validator `56d0d2eaf72f84eb2055d109477a730d`, paid synchronizer `ac070f455e4669926115acd2f508c5cd`, New Order RPC `640e8b827e05e9bc658330cedb0b2314`.
- **Required database boundary:** an application-only catalogue query/UI change is insufficient; existing trigger and synchronizer enforcement would reject inactive or unmapped selections. The narrow candidate design is a dedicated authenticated manual-New-Order RPC that verifies active Owner identity against `auth.uid()` and actor ID, sets a transaction-local marker, and delegates to the existing creation contract. Only line creation during that marked RPC would be allowed to bypass active/collection/date/customer-selectable checks; canonical names, server-side price/shorthand snapshots, and quantity limits remain enforced. Trigger authorization must independently require the marker, active Owner identity, and that the target order was created by that Owner in the same call. Existing checkout, edits, other staff RPCs, Fresh Pick, stock, and payment paths must remain unchanged. This design is proposed, not implemented or rehearsed.
- **Execution limitation:** the current local checkout is a different detached worktree and lacks the Issue #8 candidate object; a clean GitHub clone attempt could not resolve GitHub DNS. GitHub source and read-only DEV inspection were available. Exact-candidate TypeScript, lint, regression, migration-rehearsal and Preview tests were not run.
- **Decision:** do not expose the broader catalogue or deploy a Preview until a reviewed, disposable-PostgreSQL rehearsal proves the dedicated RPC/trigger boundary, Manager/staff/customer denial, selection/date-change behavior, canonical snapshots and existing checkout compatibility. No DEV migration was applied, no deployment or alias changed, and Production was not accessed.

## Issue #8 — Owner manual New Order catalogue override (2026-11-02)

**Scope:** Implemented in commit `db23f796d16975ee2e6970cbe46cf36c5668470d` (tree `a2d4842fcb040a6b0e9423f1d07cb9e07851942c`) on `codex/issue-8-addons-implementation-20261101`, parented directly on verified GitHub HEAD `5954ad40b513d8a0bb309cb02793a8be7432a3fc` (tree `53fa907e387e3b55e6751daafac212e3a423bad4`). The original Mac checkout and its uncommitted work were not modified. No hosted DEV/Production database, deployment, alias, or credential was changed.

**Application behavior:** The Owner New Order page is guarded by active Owner authorization. Its server-side catalogue reads include inactive and unmapped Complimentary Items and Paid Add-ons. Inactive items are labeled and not selected by default. The manual action alone opts into the dedicated Owner database RPC; standard staff/customer paths continue using their existing RPC and eligibility validation. Selected items remain in form state when pickup dates change. Catalogue names, paid prices, financial shorthand, quantity maximums, order totals, and snapshots are taken/validated by the database.

**Database change:** `supabase/migrations/20261102040744_issue8_owner_manual_new_order_catalogue_override.sql` — SHA-256 `5434b572faedb9d4fde620d90d6db4271ee80150c5a048288d10b20f24acca31`. It adds a dedicated authenticated Owner RPC and a transaction/backend-bound private context used only by the existing line validation/synchronization triggers. It does not add a caller-set override flag, weaken the shared trigger outside that context, change checkout RPC signatures, or alter payment, stock, allocation, or historical order logic.

**Isolated PostgreSQL 17.6 rehearsal:** A disposable, network-isolated Supabase PostgreSQL 17.6 container was reconstructed from the verified DEV logical archive's schema and migration ledger only; no customer table data was restored. The three previously approved Issue #8 migrations were replayed there in order, followed by this migration. The resulting isolated ledger had 187 rows and each of `20261101194235`, `20261101200000`, `20261101200100`, and `20261102040744` exactly once. A forced transaction error after the migration SQL and ledger insert left neither the private schema nor the new version behind; a subsequent clean application installed them once. This was a disposable rehearsal, not a hosted migration.

**SQL integration assertions:** `scripts/test-owner-manual-catalogue-override.sql` passed against the actual reconstructed function bodies, triggers and permissions, and ended with `ROLLBACK`. Active Owner creation with inactive/unmapped items passed; canonical database snapshots, paid price/shorthand, total, unpaid status and no-stock state were verified. Later catalogue edits did not change snapshots. Manager use of the override, ordinary staff, Customer Operations, non-staff, and inactive/archived Owner were denied. Manager's ordinary standard RPC remained functional; its ineligible-item submission was rejected without a partial order. Direct out-of-context trigger insertion remained rejected; paid quantity maximum and failed-transaction rollback passed. Synthetic orders/fixtures remaining after the test: zero. Read-only catalog inspection confirmed the new Owner RPC is owned by `postgres`, SECURITY DEFINER with an empty search path, executable by `authenticated` only (not `anon` or `service_role`); the internal context helper has no client-role EXECUTE grants. The pre-existing paid synchronizer ACL remains unchanged.

**Application tests:** TypeScript `npx tsc --noEmit` PASS. Scoped ESLint on the seven Owner New Order application files PASS. `node scripts/test-owner-manual-new-order-source.mjs` PASS. `node scripts/test-addon-collection-history.mjs` PASS. The existing offline release regression suite rerun completed with 57 PASS, 0 FAIL, and 1 BLOCKED; `git diff --check` and the runner’s tracked-diff hygiene check both passed. The only blocked script was the local PostgreSQL Fresh Pick assignment harness (`shmget: Operation not permitted`), not the Owner SQL rehearsal.

**Build limitation:** After replacing only the isolated checkout's external `node_modules` symlink with `npm ci` from the committed lockfile, `npm run build` reached Next.js 16.2.12 but could not fetch Google Fonts (`Newsreader` and `Outfit`) in this execution environment. No source workaround was introduced. The previous attempt's symlink-root panic was resolved by the local lockfile install.

**Unverified before any hosted DEV migration/Preview:** No hosted runtime authentication/service-role check, hosted DEV migration, Preview deployment, or browser acceptance was performed. Customer Preorder/Fresh Pick end-to-end business transactions were not created in a hosted environment. The Owner path has isolated DB integration coverage, but this is not a substitute for a DEV Preview runtime check.

**Decision:** GO for code review of the implementation and isolated database rehearsal. HOLD for hosted DEV migration or Preview until the release-regression blocked check is resolved in a compatible PostgreSQL environment, the exact-candidate build passes in an environment with Google Fonts access, and the Owner runtime is verified against DEV. ## Issue #8 Owner manual New Order — verification gate (2026-11-02)

**Locked application candidate:** `db23f796d16975ee2e6970cbe46cf36c5668470d`, tree `a2d4842fcb040a6b0e9423f1d07cb9e07851942c`. The verification workflow commit `9075bb8cb129dcb74e3808d728d39c9d9fc0b3c7` pins checkout to that exact application commit and asserts both SHA and tree before running tests. Workflow run [41390672629](https://github.com/hyphenkk3/project-flour/actions/runs/41390672629) completed successfully. The docs and workflow commits do not alter application source or the approved migration.

**Focused security review:**

- The New Order page calls `requireStaff()` and checks `role.code === 'owner'` before its service-role catalogue reads. The server action separately calls `requireOwner()`. Only this manual New Order action sets the override selector; Customer Operations and other helper callers continue using the existing standard RPC.
- The database wrapper additionally binds `auth.uid()` to the supplied staff profile and requires an active, unarchived Owner. Its function is owned by `postgres`, SECURITY DEFINER with an empty search path, and executable by `authenticated`; it is explicitly not executable by `anon` or `service_role`. The internal context helper has no client-role EXECUTE grant.
- Authorization context is stored in a private schema/table with client grants revoked and is keyed to backend PID plus transaction ID. The helper also checks the current auth user and that the order was created by that Owner. No caller-set GUC or payload flag can create the context. The wrapper invokes the existing staff order function once, then removes the context in the same transaction. Failure rolls the whole statement back.
- Shared complimentary and paid-add-on validators remain restrictive without that context. With it, the exception is limited to inactive, seasonal, unmapped, or non-customer-selectable items for that Owner-created manual order. Names, paid prices, shorthand, sort data and maximum paid quantity remain database-derived. Existing RPC signatures, customer checkout routes, stock-allocation logic, payment behavior and order snapshot update restrictions are unchanged.
- The form disables its submit button while the server action is pending. The existing order-creation API has no request idempotency key: an independently repeated valid request in a separate transaction can create a second complete unpaid order, as with the pre-existing manual path. The new exception does not change that contract or duplicate lines within one order. If exactly-once behavior across network retries is required, it needs a separately scoped idempotency change; it was not added here.

**PostgreSQL assignment regression:** The unchanged `scripts/test-extra-assignment-rpc-postgres.sh` passed against an isolated PostgreSQL 17 Docker service (17.11). The test reported `concurrent_competing_assignment=PASS` and `assignment_rpc_postgres=PASS`; it verified exactly one of two competing assignments succeeded and the stock audit and order timeline each recorded one assignment. The service container was network-disabled and removed after the test.

**Exact-candidate CI/build:** GitHub Actions run 41390672629 checked out `db23f796d16975ee2e6970cbe46cf36c5668470d`, asserted tree `a2d4842fcb040a6b0e9423f1d07cb9e07851942c`, installed its lockfile, and passed focused Owner/collection tests, the full release regression suite including the PostgreSQL assignment test, TypeScript, scoped ESLint, and `npm run build`. Run conclusion: `success`. This resolves the earlier local Google Fonts network limitation. No deployment was created.

**Additional isolated SQL rehearsal:** `scripts/test-owner-manual-catalogue-override.sql` passed in the disposable PostgreSQL 17.6 DEV-schema rehearsal described above and ended with `ROLLBACK`. It checked Owner success for inactive/unmapped items; Manager, ordinary staff, Customer Operations, non-staff, and inactive/archived Owner denial; standard Manager order compatibility; server-derived values and totals; historical snapshot preservation; paid quantity limit; rejection outside the private context; and failed-transaction rollback. Fixture rows remaining: zero. This rehearsal used schema and migration history only, not customer records.

**Current hosted DEV — read-only:** Supabase metadata and SQL identify `tzwtpxdcggesgjaqxkqr` (`project-flour-dev`), `ACTIVE_HEALTHY`, PostgreSQL `17.6.1.155`. The migration ledger has 186 rows. Versions `20261101194235`, `20261101200000`, and `20261101200100` each occur once; `20261102040744` occurs zero times. The Owner RPC and `issue8_private` schema are absent. No migration or hosted data write was performed. The approved migration SHA-256 remains `5434b572faedb9d4fde620d90d6db4271ee80150c5a048288d10b20f24acca31`.

**Recovery reference:** The existing archive remains present, mode 600, 4,950,659 bytes, SHA-256 `4c67bb6df3ad4d2fcbe7a06adb37f3781d7558fb197031b296942ec4d446d720`; its directory is mode 700 and private manifest is mode 600. Its successful PostgreSQL 17 restore is recorded in Phase 2G-R2. The archive predates the three Issue #8 migrations, so it is a tested pre-Issue8 recovery baseline, not a current point-in-time image of the 186-entry DEV ledger. Restoring it would require replaying M1–M3 and would omit any data written after its capture. For this schema-only migration, use the rehearsed atomic one-file transaction and forward recovery for post-commit issues; do not treat the archive as a lossless rollback of current DEV. No new backup was created.

**Decision:** GO to request separate authorization for the one-file DEV migration. Use the reviewed checksum and version-aware atomic runner, verify the ledger and postflight before any Preview deployment. This is not authorization to apply the migration or deploy. Hosted Owner runtime/browser acceptance remains outstanding. No Production access, migration, deployment, or alias change occurred.

# Capacity status consistency — DEV correction (2026-11-04)

**Decision: HOLD for hosted DEV execution and Production review.** The confirmed status mismatch is corrected in an isolated candidate and the PostgreSQL 17 synthetic concurrency suite passes. Hosted DEV remains unchanged because the available migration tool would write a generated migration version, while the previously qualified canonical one-file runner is not present in this execution workspace. Automatic review rejected using the generic migration tool for that reason. No workaround, SQL execution or ledger write was attempted.

- **Source baseline:** isolated branch `codex/capacity-status-consistency-20261104-review`, based exactly on accepted DEV candidate `541acb6ea9df5c6ac31b0580f0ab4b05bf2b28e3`, tree `b19bfba6f67f732a5493c2c644309cc41e4f5933`. The prior candidate and its two capacity serialization migrations remain unchanged.
- **Status rule:** guest checkout inserts `submitted`; staff can move it through `pending_confirmation` to `confirmed`, then `awaiting_payment` and `paid`. The guest reservation and deferred integrity checks already counted `submitted`, `pending_confirmation`, `awaiting_payment`, and `paid`, but omitted `confirmed`. The forward-only correction adds `confirmed` to that same five-function set. `cancelled` and `completed` release capacity. Fresh Pick remains outside preorder capacity by its existing `extra_stock_id` exclusion.
- **Capacity-setting semantics:** the existing staff “confirmed floor” remains `confirmed`, `awaiting_payment`, and `paid`, matching its documented UI/business meaning. The authoritative deferred capacity constraint uses the full active reservation set, including submitted and pending orders and active unexpired waiting-list holds. Therefore a reduction that would undercut either a confirmed commitment or a still-active reservation aborts transactionally; no order status meaning or staff floor display was silently changed.
- **Migration:** `supabase/migrations/20261104001213_capacity_status_consistency.sql`, SHA-256 `68bf1f61f463ae250238207b758106ac67f8c7df20d80a570c0a42e88867c7d4`. It refuses to run unless all five DEV functions match the reviewed post-M1/M2 body checksums, owners, `SECURITY DEFINER`, search paths, status-list counts and non-client EXECUTE grants. It replaces only the status-list text in those definitions and postflights all five functions plus the eight enabled lock/deferred-check triggers. It does not alter M1/M2 or their checksums, RLS, grants, lock keys, retryable `40001` behavior, payment logic, or Fresh Pick allocation.
- **Local PostgreSQL 17.11 rehearsal:** `scripts/test-capacity-concurrency-postgres.sh` applied the two existing migrations and this candidate migration to an isolated temporary cluster. Independent-session tests passed: one-slot competition (one succeeds), sufficient capacity, independent dates, submitted-to-confirmed retention, rejection when a confirmed order occupies the slot, capacity reductions blocked below pending and confirmed orders, concurrent status-transition `40001` plus successful retry, active waiting-list hold, staff-created order validation, cancellation release, date-change rollback, failed checkout rollback, lock-conflict behavior, and Fresh Pick isolation. The cluster was removed by the test cleanup. This uses the actual capacity helper/trigger bodies from the migrations and synthetic RPC wrappers; it is not a full hosted guest RPC execution.
- **DEV read-only compatibility:** Supabase project `tzwtpxdcggesgjaqxkqr`, PostgreSQL 17.6, database/role `postgres`. Ledger is 189 entries; `20261103140120` and `20261103140631` each appear once; `20261104001213` is absent. All five function checksums/security properties match the migration preconditions, and the existing `submit_guest_preorder` definition calls `_guest_preorder_item_fully_booked`. No DEV object or data was changed. This is the exact verified starting point for the migration, but post-migration hosted RPC execution remains unverified.
- **Production read-only comparison:** Supabase project `cuowwlduhxxifmpvpltt`, PostgreSQL 17.6, ledger 183, with Issue #8 versions M1–M4 each recorded once. Production is still before the two capacity-serialization migrations; its `set_production_capacity` and guest checkout definitions are the pre-M1 forms. The M3 correction is therefore only valid after the two capacity migrations, in order. Production was read only and remains unchanged. The separate staff confirmed-floor function currently uses the three documented statuses.
- **Verification:** `npm run test:release-regressions` — 64 PASS, 0 FAIL, 0 BLOCKED, including the PostgreSQL assignment and concurrency harnesses. Application TypeScript and release-regression TypeScript — PASS. Scoped ESLint — PASS. `git diff --check` — PASS. Exact-candidate GitHub Actions has not yet run.
- **Remaining gates:** obtain the already-qualified canonical one-file transactional runner in this workspace (or its exact checksum-verified assets and procedure) before any DEV migration; then apply this file as the next canonical version, verify its actual recorded version and postflight, and exercise the real hosted DEV guest RPC using isolated rollback-only data with external effects ruled out. The current generated file version is the intended canonical version if the qualified runner supports it. ## Staff Web, Sound & Push Notifications — local implementation update (2026-11-09)

**Outcome: LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION PARTIAL; no hosted changes.** This entry supersedes the implementation-state description above while preserving it as the earlier checkpoint. The earlier sound and push design decisions remain in force.

- **Identity and isolation:** working branch `codex/staff-notifications-dev-20261109` remains based on exact approved application commit `096ae0deae864ee050210b33e4f8aa99ae7e10f5`, tree `0722a10e0e1602deacee40d751e6a974ae596ddd`. The branch is still uncommitted and local. No hosted DEV or Production database, Vercel deployment, environment variable, domain, order, payment, stock or customer data was accessed or changed. The original approved-source checkout, Issue #9 worktree and recovery evidence were left untouched.
- **Web and Email:** existing notification events, recipients, classifications, web event stream and email dispatch remain the source of truth. The existing `new_order` event continues to apply only to eligible normal preorder inserts; no Fresh Pick event eligibility was broadened. Existing email delivery remains separate. Push dispatch was added as an independent post-response callback and to the existing authenticated dispatch sweep; dispatch uses `Promise.allSettled`, so push failure does not suppress email and neither channel is part of a checkout transaction. If the post-response hook is unavailable, Push waits for the existing sweep rather than running synchronously inside an order action.
- **Sound:** retained the local default-off `new_order` sound preference, explicit-gesture audio priming, quiet two-note Web Audio cue, and silent fallback for browser/audio restrictions. It uses the existing new-event listener and cross-tab event claim, does not fetch history or replay old events after login/reconnect, and does not change event eligibility.
- **Push implementation:** added staff-only server actions, current-browser subscription management, strict HTTPS push-provider host validation, service-role-only subscription storage and delivery RPCs, a generic service worker with push/click handlers only (no fetch/cache/offline behavior), VAPID sender using lockfile-pinned `web-push` 4.0.0, and bounded retry/revocation handling. Payloads contain only an event ID and existing notification code; the worker renders fixed generic text and a fixed internal route, with no customer/order details. Queue uniqueness is `(event_id, subscription_id)`; the worker uses the event ID as notification tag with `renotify: false`. Each dispatch claims at most 10 deliveries concurrently, each provider socket timeout is 8 seconds, expired subscriptions and HTTP 404/410 endpoints are revoked, and retry delays are bounded. Preference opt-in time and subscription creation time prevent delivery of events from before opt-in/subscription. Staff deactivation, archival, and role changes disable Push preferences and revoke subscriptions in the same database transaction; if revocation cannot be written, the staff-profile change fails closed rather than leaving an old opt-in active.
- **Draft migrations (not applied or PostgreSQL-rehearsed):** sound preference migration `supabase/migrations/20261109110501_staff_notification_sound_preference.sql`, SHA-256 `a4896ed87b0e621dbb0bdbb4f82e52e3dcc0d3296ec5bd0ece7baf21354e5dbf`; Push preference, subscription, queue, claim, completion, revocation and security migration `supabase/migrations/20261109193000_staff_notification_push.sql`, SHA-256 `3b4ac6f19706bf9356500abfddf478cc14b09e25275162448a5b474d30904339`. The Push migration adds only notification preference/queue objects and triggers; it does not alter checkout, order, payment, stock, fulfilment, existing notification generation, or business records. New Push tables have RLS enabled, no client policies, and direct table grants revoked from `public`, `anon`, and `authenticated`; server-side functions are restricted to `service_role`. SQL syntax, grants, trigger behavior, atomicity and rollback remain **NOT VERIFIED by PostgreSQL**, because disposable PostgreSQL initialization is blocked in this environment.
- **Changed source and test files:** `package.json`, `package-lock.json`; `scripts/test-release-regressions.mjs`, `scripts/test-staff-notifications.ts`, `scripts/test-staff-notification-sound.ts`, `scripts/test-staff-push-notifications.ts`; `src/app/(app)/settings/page.tsx`, `src/app/api/staff/notifications/dispatch/route.ts`; `src/components/settings/NotificationPreferences.tsx`, `src/components/shell/StaffNotificationListener.tsx`; `src/foundation/auth/actions.ts`; `src/foundation/staff/notification-event-identity.ts`, `notification-preferences.ts`, `notification-preferences-actions.ts`, `notification-preferences-queries.ts`, `schedule-staff-notification-dispatch.ts`; new `src/foundation/staff/staff-notification-sound.ts`, `staff-notification-push-actions.ts`, `staff-notification-push-client.ts`, `staff-notification-push-delivery-core.ts`, `staff-notification-push-dispatch.ts`, `staff-notification-push-protocol.ts`, `staff-notification-push-storage.ts`; new `src/types/web-push.d.ts`, `public/sw.js`, and the two draft migrations listed above. No checkout, order, inventory, payment, fulfilment RPC or workflow source file was changed.
- **Local tests — final full run:** after the final migration and delivery changes, `npm run test:release-regressions` completed **68 PASS, 0 FAIL, 3 BLOCKED**. Web/email/sound and Push mocked transport/privacy/retry contracts passed; application TypeScript, regression-test TypeScript, changed-test ESLint and tracked diff hygiene passed. Focused Push test `node --import ./scripts/test-release-regression-alias-loader.mjs scripts/test-staff-push-notifications.ts` passed. Scoped ESLint passed with zero errors and one existing `_role` unused-variable warning in `notification-preferences.ts`; Prettier check passed. The three blocked suites are disposable PostgreSQL Fresh Pick assignment, capacity serialization/rollback, and canonical migration-runner mechanics. Each failed in `initdb` before application assertions because the environment denied `shmget` shared-memory creation (`Operation not permitted`); Docker is unavailable. No test assertion was weakened, and blocked checks are not counted as passed.
- **Build:** `npm run build` is **BLOCKED** on the exact current source because the environment could not fetch the existing Google Fonts `Newsreader` and `Outfit` resources from `fonts.googleapis.com`. No font, dependency, or build workaround was made. This follows the permitted offline environment limitation, not a demonstrated source build defect.
- **Runtime and delivery gates:** no VAPID keys or subject are configured; the settings page therefore shows “Push setup needed” and dispatch returns `not_configured`. ### Read-only function grant detail for the same preflight

A further catalog-only query inspected signatures, owner, `SECURITY DEFINER`, function settings, and effective `EXECUTE` for all 17 existing `public.staff_notification%` functions. All are owned by `postgres`; 15 are `SECURITY DEFINER`, use `search_path=public` except the two email test-scope helpers whose configured path is empty, and all return `has_function_privilege(service_role, ..., EXECUTE)=true`. The two non-definer helpers are `staff_notification_email_delivery_is_claimable(...)` and `staff_notification_email_retry_delay(integer)`. Effective `anon`/`authenticated` EXECUTE was false for the non-trigger helper/API functions. Six trigger routines (`staff_notification_emit_new_order_row`, `staff_notification_emit_waiting_list_new_request_row`, `staff_notification_on_approvals`, `staff_notification_on_orders`, `staff_notification_on_timeline`, `staff_notification_request_dispatch`) show effective EXECUTE for `anon`/`authenticated`; these are trigger-returning routines and cannot be invoked as ordinary functions outside trigger context. No invocation or data operation was attempted. The Push migration explicitly revokes client EXECUTE on its new SECURITY DEFINER RPCs and grants those RPCs to `service_role` only. This is metadata evidence, not an adversarial authorization test.

It reproduced the hosted preference table's three `authenticated` own-profile policies (SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE with `auth.uid()` and `is_active=true` checks), RLS enablement, and direct `ALL` privileges for `anon`, `authenticated`, `service_role`, and `postgres`. The resulting preference ACL text exactly matched before and after the notification migrations: `{postgres=arwdDxtm/postgres,anon=arwdDxtm/postgres,authenticated=arwdDxtm/postgres,service_role=arwdDxtm/postgres}`. Local roles had no memberships; prior hosted metadata confirmed `anon` is not a member of `authenticated`, but the reverse membership was not independently captured in this offline-only task. The fixture included active Owner/Bakery, inactive and archived synthetic staff, a non-staff identity, Web/Email settings and one synthetic email-delivery row. The 193-entry ledger fixture used synthetic prior-version placeholders plus the verified current DEV latest version/name marker; it was not an exact 193-version manifest reconstruction.
- **RLS behavior:** before and after migration, row-level SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE were denied for `anon`, authenticated non-staff, inactive and archived staff. Active staff could read/update/insert only their own preference rows; cross-staff row reads/updates were unavailable and DELETE had no applicable policy. The service role retained its intended bypass/access. These SQL-role simulations used the repository policy definitions and synthetic identities.
- **Confirmed privilege exposure:** because RLS does not govern `TRUNCATE`, both `anon` and authenticated non-staff successfully truncated the synthetic preference table under their effective grants. Each isolated probe ran within a savepoint/transaction and rolled back, restoring all synthetic rows. Thus `ANONYMOUS ACCESS` and `NON-STAFF ACCESS` are **EXPOSED at the PostgreSQL role privilege level**, and cross-staff destructive isolation is **EXPOSED** through that global operation. The notification migrations did not create or widen this pre-existing grant, and the exact preference ACL remained unchanged. This does not prove that an ordinary PostgREST client can submit arbitrary `TRUNCATE`; that API reachability was not tested. No hosted table was altered. A least-privilege grant correction requires separate review and authorization; none was made.
- **Migration result:** both unchanged migrations applied successfully in order in one `psql --single-transaction` run with a transaction advisory lock, 3-second lock timeout, 5-minute statement timeout, exact target version/name absence guard, and canonical `statements text[]` arrays. Synthetic history advanced 193→195, with exactly one Sound entry (1 canonical statement) and one Push entry (32 statements). Postflight retained the existing Web/Email values and email-delivery fixture; new Web/Email defaults remained enabled, Sound/Push defaults were off, and the opt-in timestamp was initially null. Existing preference ACL text was byte-for-byte unchanged. Push tables had RLS enabled and no client table privileges; new Push RPC execute privileges were denied to `anon`/`authenticated` and granted to `service_role`.
- **Focused behavior results:** active synthetic staff changed only their own Sound/Push preferences and received the expected `push_enabled_at`; cross-staff updates affected zero rows. Active subscription registration passed; inactive registration and endpoint transfer to another staff account were denied. A newly eligible synthetic event queued once, its duplicate queued zero times, claim/completion passed, and staff deactivation disabled Push and revoked its subscription. The post-migration behavior script ended with `ROLLBACK`; final synthetic state retained four baseline preference rows and one existing email row, with zero subscription/delivery rows and the owner still active.

Read-only postflight showed ledger still 193, no target entries, no Push tables and none of the three new preference columns. The duplicate target guard returned expected exit 3 on the successfully migrated rehearsal database; read-only postflight remained at 195 with the single exact target entries and statement-array cardinalities unchanged. The first pre-migration ACL script run also exited nonzero because its service-role row-count assertion expected four after the same test had inserted a fifth authorized synthetic row. That harness expectation was corrected to five (not weakened); the rerun passed. Both initial and corrected logs are retained.
- **Business-object scope:** static review of both migration files found no DDL/DML targeting checkout, orders, stock, payment, capacity or fulfilment objects. The focused database fixture did not include those business schemas, so this is migration-scope review, not full business-workflow integration testing.

## 2026-11-11 — iPhone Push readiness and category preference audit

Read-only audit of the existing clean Staff Notifications Preview and its exact source: Vercel deployment `dpl_CbZcEoB3bRMQ7bTTi7f5ZSeBd9jh`, READY at `project-flour-7dyc320r8-hyphen-org.vercel.app`, branch `codex/staff-notifications-dev-20261109`, source commit `31dff289971c2f25d8dbb8a76bc1da87115b0f16`, project `prj_3rmlAQzLSFOi15DEGczSuDYDq8xr`. Read-only Vercel metadata showed all seven Push/Supabase variables present for Preview and targeted only to that exact branch; the VAPID public key is present, and private VAPID and dispatch values are Secret. No secret values were decrypted or recorded. Existing runtime evidence continues to identify the public Supabase endpoint as DEV `tzwtpxdcggesgjaqxkqr`. The stored DEV service-role credential's project identity remains unverified; this was not re-probed.

**iPhone/PWA source readiness:** `src/app/manifest.ts` provides a standalone web app, root start URL, and valid 192×192 and 512×512 PNG icons. `public/sw.js` handles Push display and notification clicks only; it has no fetch/cache/offline handler. The client registers `/sw.js` at `/` only on Push opt-in, requests notification permission from the staff button gesture, and subscribes through the browser Push API using the configured public VAPID key. The worker displays fixed generic text and internal destinations, not customer/order details. Apple documents Web Push for Home Screen web apps on iOS 16.4 or later and requires permission to be requested from a user gesture; actual iPhone installation, permission, foreground/background, and closed-app delivery were not tested. No recurring Push scheduler is configured: `vercel.json` contains only the function region, and the application dispatch is post-response/event-driven after a trusted mutation.

**Subscription authorization design:** registration and revocation server actions call `requireStaff`; the privileged registration RPC checks the supplied authenticated staff profile is active and unarchived, limits each staff member to ten subscriptions, and prevents endpoint reassignment across staff. Subscription/delivery tables deny client-role table grants and are service-role-only. Delivery selection rechecks active/non-archived staff, per-category opt-in time, and subscription ownership/age. The logout action revokes that staff member's subscriptions and Push preferences; a staff-profile trigger does so on deactivation, archival, or role change. This is source/migration review, not a new hosted subscription test. Because the exact Preview service-role credential identity remains unverified, live subscription persistence is still a runtime **HOLD** despite these code-level controls.

**Current notification catalogue and preference matrix:** the existing catalogue has nine codes: `new_order`, `order_paid`, `order_confirmed`, `order_cancelled`, `order_edited`, `approval_required`, `last_minute`, `fresh_pick_walk_in_hold_reminder`, and `waiting_list_new_request`. Web controls are rendered independently for all nine. Push controls and `push_enabled` persistence are per staff/category for all nine, default off; Push delivery joins the event code to that staff/category preference. Sound is default off and currently exposed, saved, and honored only for `new_order`: the settings UI renders the Sound switch only for that code, the server action only writes `sound_enabled` for it, and `shouldPlayStaffNotificationSound` rejects every other code. Thus the database's per-staff/category preference rows do not currently provide per-category Sound behavior. There are no separate collection-update or delivery-update codes in this catalogue; no new categories or eligibility rules were inferred or added. Existing normal new-order rules still exclude Fresh Pick stock-linked orders.

**Decision:** per-category Web preferences **PRESENT**; per-category Push preferences **PRESENT**; per-category Sound preferences **PARTIAL** (new-order only). Smallest implementation scope, if separately authorized: reuse the existing per-staff/category `sound_enabled` column; expose the Sound toggle on each existing category row, persist it for each validated category, and allow the sound predicate to honor the event's own category preference; add focused preference/action/eligibility tests. No schema migration appears necessary from the reviewed current schema. This audit made no source, database, Vercel, deployment, Push subscription, notification, Email, or Production changes. Email remains paused with its hidden toggle and HTTP 503 contract. No synthetic orders or notifications were created. Production impact: **NONE**. ## 2026-11-11 — iPhone Push safety and credential dependency review

Read-only review of source at branch `codex/staff-notifications-dev-20261109`, commit `ceb32bb662bb34f8b5bee9b90f6597f3aa5f3133`, and deployment metadata for Preview `dpl_9oM3tg8zsNHrE7Bm3phDuuvwukNF` confirmed the existing Push chain. The browser requests notification permission after the staff member's opt-in action, obtains a browser Push subscription through the root service worker and the public VAPID key, then calls the authenticated server action `registerStaffPushSubscriptionAction`. That action verifies the active staff session and calls `saveStaffPushSubscription`, which constructs `createServiceClient()` and invokes `register_staff_notification_push_subscription`. The migration revokes that RPC from `PUBLIC`, `anon`, and `authenticated`, granting execution only to `service_role`. Thus browser subscription creation alone does not register the subscription in DEV; the existing registration flow requires the service-role credential.

Push opt-out and staff-wide revocation also use `createServiceClient()` to update preference/subscription rows. The migration's inactive/archived/role-change trigger disables Push eligibility, and dispatch eligibility additionally checks active staff, current category preference, subscription state, and event/subscription timestamps. Delivery uses `createServiceClient()` for `queue_staff_notification_push_deliveries`, `claim_staff_notification_push_deliveries`, and `complete_staff_notification_push_delivery`, each restricted to `service_role`; it then sends the claimed payload through Web Push using server-side VAPID credentials. The authenticated `/api/staff/notifications/push-dispatch` route additionally checks `STAFF_PUSH_DISPATCH_SECRET`, but that secret does not replace service-role authorization and dispatch can send to eligible subscriptions. The post-response dispatcher is independent of paused Email and invokes the same service-role delivery path; no recurring scheduler was found in the reviewed flow. No dispatcher or external Push provider was invoked in this review.

The separate preference read/save workflow uses the normal authenticated Supabase server client and RLS. Its prior successful hosted persistence is not evidence for the service-role key, because it exercises a different credential and grant path. Therefore an iPhone may create a local browser subscription after opt-in, but the existing app cannot safely complete staff subscription registration without the verified DEV service-role path. There is no existing Push delivery route that avoids service-role calls; using the dispatch route would also risk sending to eligible subscriptions and is not an acceptable credential test.

Read-only Vercel metadata for Preview deployment `dpl_9oM3tg8zsNHrE7Bm3phDuuvwukNF` reconfirmed the exact branch and commit, Preview target, and generated branch-only alias. The branch-filtered metadata reported zero hidden Production-scoped variables. The Supabase URL and key values were not retrieved. These scopes constrain these bindings to Preview deployments for this branch; they do not attest the service-role key's project identity, nor prove that any public URL value alone establishes server-side isolation. No Vercel configuration or deployment was changed.

Decision: **C — HOLD**. No evidence indicates that the stored key is wrong, so replacement is not justified. ## 2026-11-11 — Push notification click destinations corrected locally

The Owner-confirmed iPhone tap-through failure was reproduced by source inspection: `public/sw.js` and `staff-notification-push-protocol.ts` used `/owner/orders` without an index page and `/owner/waiting-list` without a route. Existing order notifications do not carry an order ID through the Push claim/payload flow (only the event ID and category are sent), so the safe New Order destination is the existing `/owner` operations dashboard. The corrected category mapping is: `new_order`, `order_paid`, `order_confirmed`, `order_cancelled`, `order_edited`, and `last_minute` → `/owner`; `approval_required` → `/owner/approvals`; `fresh_pick_walk_in_hold_reminder` → `/customer-operations/fresh-picks`; `waiting_list_new_request` → `/bakery/availability#waiting-list-heading`. These pages exist in the authenticated `(app)` route group and retain their existing staff and role-capability checks. Waiting List navigation uses only the stable page/section path; it does not put request or customer details in Push data.

The worker now resolves the route against its own origin and refuses cross-origin notification targets. The root `/sw.js` registration URL, `/` scope, update cache behavior, Push subscription, VAPID configuration, event eligibility, dispatch, payload, Web notifications, Sound, and duplicate delivery behavior are unchanged. A changed worker script is eligible for the browser's normal service-worker update on the existing registration; closing and reopening the installed DEV PWA after a later approved Preview deployment lets iOS complete the update without changing its Push subscription. No hosted deployment or device action was performed here.

Focused service-worker tests execute the actual `public/sw.js` with synthetic events and cover all nine mappings, route-file existence, authentication/authorization guards, same-origin navigation when a window is open, opening the route when no app window exists, and refusal of a cross-origin target. Push, Web, and Sound regressions passed. TypeScript, focused ESLint, Prettier, and diff hygiene passed. The local production build was attempted once but is **BLOCKED** in this restricted execution environment because Next.js could not fetch the existing Google Fonts (Newsreader and Outfit); no font or build configuration was changed. Running the same build from Mac Terminal with the already-connected hotspot may provide the required Google Fonts connectivity. No new orders, subscriptions, or Push sends occurred. Email remains disabled, hidden, and paused with HTTP 503 behavior unchanged. No Production system was accessed or changed. **Production impact: NONE.**

The focused local source/test commit is `7d66401f4d71d8a456bb05e6894ff7f54fde7904` on `codex/staff-notifications-dev-20261109`; it contains only `public/sw.js`, the Push protocol destination map, and the focused Push regression test. This commit was not pushed or deployed. The Master Status append remains in the existing uncommitted documentation change so earlier local evidence is preserved.

## 2026-11-11 — DEV notification reliability acceptance: Fresh Pick hold reminder

The Owner authorized a synthetic DEV Fresh Pick walk-in hold test, including a temporary Fresh Pick reminder Sound/Push opt-in, one possible Push to the existing Owner iPhone subscription, and restoration of preferences. No Production project, URL, database, customer data, or setting was accessed.

Using the authenticated Owner → Bakery → EXTRA workflow, a synthetic Avocado 6-inch unit was created with note `DEV ONLY — synthetic Fresh Pick reminder test; not for sale`, stock ID `8935b461-71f0-4ed2-91ca-cc8d72bc1336`. Its normal Owner Walk-in Hold action started one 15-minute hold at `2026-11-11 07:04:48 UTC` (15:04:48 Malaysia) through `07:19:48 UTC`. A pre-existing attempt ID `0b2ccc3b-ffd2-4953-b367-a8050ba0169e` had already been created with its cutoff at 15:00 Malaysia and was not held; it is retained as expired synthetic test stock. No customer order, payment or external message was created.

The active DEV cron sweep ran on its normal schedule. Exactly one `fresh_pick_walk_in_hold_reminder` event was recorded for the test stock at `2026-11-11 07:17:00.468737 UTC` (15:17:00 Malaysia), 12 minutes 12 seconds after hold start and approximately 12 seconds after the expected `held_until - 3 minutes` threshold. The reminder event was still exactly one after subsequent sweep time, supporting duplicate suppression for this hold. The authenticated staff page displayed the Fresh Pick hold Web toast. The Owner confirmed hearing one short sound from the laptop. No browser-side error was reported. The held unit expired normally; a subsequent read-only check showed no active hold and no sale. The reminder marker was cleared by expiry cleanup while the event record remained. The test stock remains retained in DEV through its configured cutoff; no bulk cleanup or direct database write was used.

Push was opted in only for the Owner's Fresh Pick reminder category during the test, with one active DEV subscription and zero pre-existing pending/claimed deliveries. After the sweep, the test event had **zero** Push delivery rows; no Push provider attempt or iPhone delivery occurred. Source review confirms the gap: `scheduleStaffNotificationDispatch` starts Push dispatch after application server actions, while `sweep_extra_walk_in_hold_reminders` is invoked by the database cron job and does not call the application dispatcher. The Push endpoint is separately protected by its dispatch secret. No manual endpoint call or unrelated queue dispatch was made. This is a confirmed scheduled-event-to-Push handoff gap, not a reminder-generation, Web delivery, or Sound playback failure.

After the test, the Owner's Fresh Pick Push preference was restored to OFF; Fresh Pick Web remained ON and Sound remained ON as before the test. A read-only DEV check confirmed one active subscription, zero pending/claimed deliveries, Fresh Pick preferences `{web:true, sound:true, push:false}`, and no event delivery row. No subscription details, secret values, or personal data were selected. Email remained disabled/hidden/HTTP 503 paused; no Email provider was invoked.

### Nine-category evidence boundary

Source inspection identified these existing event producers: `new_order` — qualifying order insert trigger; `order_paid` — qualifying paid transition trigger; `order_confirmed` and `order_edited` — order timeline transitions; `order_cancelled` and `last_minute` — qualifying order transitions; `approval_required` — pending operations approval request; `fresh_pick_walk_in_hold_reminder` — scheduled Fresh Pick reminder sweep; `waiting_list_new_request` — qualifying waiting-list request insert, excluding staff-created requests. Existing category controls and stored per-category preferences cover all nine categories. This run did not exercise the remaining seven non-New-Order categories end-to-end, so their event delivery, Sound, Push, recipient, and navigation outcomes remain NOT TESTED. Prior New-Order Web/Sound/iPhone Push/tap acceptance remains preserved and was not repeated. The previously deferred same-account cross-tab Sound duplicate concern was not retested.

The existing Web listener receives `staff_notification_events` INSERTs via Supabase Realtime; this test's visible toast confirms one live event reached the signed-in Owner. Source inspection shows the listener logs channel error/timeout but does not replay missed events after disconnect or refresh; therefore disconnect recovery is not verified and events during a disconnect may be missed. No polling or architecture change was introduced.

**Disposition:** Fresh Pick reminder generation/schedule/Web/Sound passed this single DEV case; scheduled Push dispatch for that event did not run and is a confirmed defect. The nine-category system is not fully accepted. Smallest proposed fix: securely connect the cron-generated reminder's exact event ID to the existing Push dispatcher, with no broad drain of unrelated events; requires separate implementation authorization. Further category-by-category receiver tests remain outstanding. Production impact: **NONE**.

## 2026-11-11 — Fresh Pick hold reminder Push handoff candidate (local only)

Root cause confirmed from the existing event path: `pg_cron` invokes `sweep_extra_walk_in_hold_reminders()` every minute; that function emits the durable `fresh_pick_walk_in_hold_reminder` event and marks the current hold reminder as emitted. Realtime delivers the same event to Web/Sound. Push delivery is only started by application actions through `scheduleStaffNotificationDispatch()`/`after()`. The database sweep cannot run that Vercel server code, and no scheduled server-side Push worker was present. This matches the live DEV evidence: one reminder event, Web and laptop Sound observed, zero Push delivery rows. This was a handoff/scheduler gap, not reminder generation.

Prepared a local-only implementation candidate. A new Preview-only server route is fail-closed on `VERCEL_ENV=preview`, the exact notification Git branch, the exact DEV Supabase URL, and a timing-safe `CRON_SECRET` bearer check. It retrieves a bounded list of due Fresh Pick reminder event UUIDs from a service-role-only RPC, validates and deduplicates every ID, confirms each event's category, and dispatches each one through the existing event-scoped Push queue. Missing, malformed, duplicate, unknown, and non-reminder IDs cannot invoke the generic unscoped dispatcher. The existing general Push endpoint and its callers are unchanged.

The candidate migration adds the service-role-only due-event query, applies the existing recipient/preference/subscription rules, retains event/subscription uniqueness and the existing retry schedule, and adds terminal `suppressed` handling for stale holds. It uses the existing lock-and-compare validator immediately before Push send; replacement/extension, release, sale/cut, expiry, missing stock, or malformed payload suppresses the stale delivery. A transient pre-send validation or provider failure remains retryable while the same hold is still current; once the hold is stale, it is terminally suppressed. The existing 15-minute hold and 3-minute reminder constants and database sweep schedule were not changed.

Local files prepared: `src/app/api/staff/notifications/fresh-pick-reminder-dispatch/route.ts`; `src/foundation/staff/fresh-pick-push-handoff.ts`; targeted changes to `src/foundation/staff/staff-notification-push-dispatch.ts` and `staff-notification-push-delivery-core.ts`; candidate migration `supabase/migrations/20261111120000_staff_notification_fresh_pick_push_handoff.sql`; focused Push assertions in `scripts/test-staff-push-notifications.ts`; and a disposable PostgreSQL fixture at `scripts/test-fresh-pick-push-handoff-postgres.sql`. No email code, existing Push endpoint, Vercel schedule, environment variable, migration ledger, deployment, or database was changed.

Focused Push handoff and delivery tests passed, covering missing/malformed/duplicate candidate IDs (zero unscoped calls), valid event-scoped dispatch selection, stale pre-send suppression without provider invocation, retry after transient preflight failure, existing provider retry/recipient/dedup contracts, and the Email 503/hidden-toggle freeze assertions. Existing Web notifications and Sound preference regressions passed; the existing Fresh Pick hold reminder regression passed. TypeScript, focused ESLint, Prettier, and `git diff --check` passed. The production build remains blocked by this execution environment's inability to fetch the repository's existing Google Fonts (Newsreader and Outfit). The disposable PostgreSQL 17.11 test could not be started: `initdb` failed because this execution environment denies SysV shared-memory creation (`Operation not permitted`); Docker socket access is also denied. The SQL fixture was not run, and no tests were weakened. No hosted synthetic order or Push was created or sent.

Operational status remains **HOLD**. A recurring server invocation is required for automatic delivery/retry; this task intentionally did not change cron configuration. The route requires a notification-branch-scoped `CRON_SECRET` and exact Vercel Preview branch runtime identity. Neither Preview scheduling support nor this variable was changed or verified here. The candidate migration is not applied. Do not publish/deploy this candidate or apply the migration until these constraints and the isolated SQL qualification are reviewed and separately authorized. A future DEV release must apply the candidate migration only to DEV, configure a branch-only schedule/secret without activating a Production job, verify the exact Preview runtime guards, and then conduct one Owner-authorized synthetic hold test while restoring temporary preferences and cleaning up test data. Production impact: **NONE**; Email remains disabled, hidden, and paused with the HTTP 503 contract preserved.

## 2026-11-11 — Fresh Pick reminder Push handoff qualification (local only)

**Root cause confirmed:** the DEV `pg_cron` sweep emits the Fresh Pick walk-in hold reminder event and Realtime serves Web/Sound, while Push dispatch is started only by application server actions. There was no safe server-side bridge from the scheduled database event to Vercel's existing Push sender. This matches the prior DEV acceptance evidence: one reminder event, Web and laptop Sound observed, zero Push delivery rows.

**Architecture selected:** a one-minute Vercel static Schedule targets a dedicated server route. Current Vercel documentation states static schedules on Preview branches start inactive and can be enabled for an exact Git branch for a bounded window; each run targets that branch's current deployment. The route accepts only an authorization bearer matching `CRON_SECRET`, then independently fails closed unless `VERCEL_ENV=preview`, the Git branch is exactly `codex/staff-notifications-dev-20261109`, and the configured Supabase URL is exactly the DEV project. It queries at most five oldest due Fresh Pick reminder event IDs, validates/deduplicates each ID, and calls the existing dispatcher with that specific ID only. The route never invokes the generic unscoped queue drain. It suppresses stale pending/failed/expired-claim rows, revalidates the exact active hold immediately before provider send, reports sanitized aggregate counters, and returns a failure response for infrastructure/completion errors. Existing Push delivery is bounded to ten subscriptions per event, with an eight-second provider timeout and the existing five-attempt retry policy. Five sequential events fit the route's declared 60-second maximum under the per-event timeout bound. A dedicated notification-branch Preview `CRON_SECRET` is a release prerequisite; no secret was created or changed here. Vercel's currently installed CLI (59.3.0) does not expose the documented `vercel schedules` command, so schedule activation was not attempted. No scheduler is activated.

**Disposable PostgreSQL qualification:** the exact candidate migration (`supabase/migrations/20261111120000_staff_notification_fresh_pick_push_handoff.sql`, SHA-256 `7f21442519475cf9ea6736fc7b3c2839495a6df7bad86ef420e4e60006cc4a57`) passed the isolated fixture on local PostgreSQL 17.11, matching DEV's PostgreSQL 17 major version (DEV is 17.6). The cluster was temporary, local-only, Unix-socket-only (`listen_addresses=''`), host authentication rejected, and removed on exit. The fixture verified rollback, repeat application, active due reminder selection, Owner-enabled vs Push-disabled recipient filtering, service-role-only/SECURITY DEFINER RPC permissions, exact-event queue idempotence, unrelated-event exclusion, transient provider failure and retry, stale pre-send suppression, cleanup of stale pending rows, and extension/release/sale/expiry/replacement invalidation. No hosted database was contacted. The minor-version difference remains a limitation; hosted DEV migration remains unapplied.

**Application qualification:** focused Push handoff/delivery, Web notification, Sound preference, and existing Fresh Pick reminder regression tests passed. TypeScript, focused ESLint, Prettier, and `git diff --check` passed. `npm run build` completed successfully using the host network for the repository's existing Google Fonts. No real Push, order, or customer communication was used. Email source/configuration was not changed; frozen HTTP 503/hidden-toggle assertions continue to pass.

Changed candidate files are limited to: `vercel.json`; the new scheduled handoff route and event-ID validator; targeted Push dispatch/delivery behavior for exact-event dispatch, pre-send stale validation, failure reporting, and suppression; the candidate migration; focused Push/notification regression assertions; the disposable PostgreSQL fixture; and this appended evidence. The general Push endpoint, Push registration, category preferences, reminder timing/hold duration, existing database sweep, Email behavior, checkout, payment, stock, fulfilment, hosted environments and aliases remain unchanged.

**Disposition:** local qualification **PASS**. Candidate source commit created locally only; GitHub publication, DEV migration, Preview deployment, branch-only `CRON_SECRET` configuration and schedule activation remain **HOLD** for separate authorization. Before release, configure and verify the dedicated Preview branch secret, publish/deploy only the exact notification branch under the existing skip protection using an approved per-deployment path, apply this migration only to DEV, verify the exact Preview function runtime/max-duration support, and enable `staff-fresh-pick-reminder-push` only for the notification branch for a bounded test window. Then conduct a separate Owner-authorized synthetic DEV hold test. No production scheduler, credential, database, deployment, alias, or customer system was accessed or changed. Email remains frozen. **Production impact: NONE.**

## 2026-11-12 — DEV Web notification recovery and same-profile tab deduplication candidate

- **Scope:** local-only work on the notification branch. No Supabase request, migration, hosted setting, scheduler, Push dispatch, order, or deployment was performed. Fresh Pick schedule remains Disabled. Existing Web/Sound/New Order/Fresh Pick acceptance was not repeated.
- **Recovery design:** the listener now runs a recipient-scoped, RLS-protected `SELECT` against `staff_notification_events` after every Realtime `SUBSCRIBED` (initial connection and reconnect). It uses the browser Supabase client, not a service-role client; the active-staff SELECT RLS policy is unchanged. The query selects only event fields needed for the existing toast, orders by `(created_at,id)`, and bounds both ends of the recovery window to the last 15 minutes and current time. The page size is 25; at most five rows are presented per pass, after which an explicit Retry recovery control resumes from the last saved cursor. The cursor is per staff ID in local storage, persisted after each processed event, and survives refresh. Cursor overlap and live/recovery overlap use the same event-ID claim. No periodic polling or Push dispatcher call is added.
- **Preferences and stale backlog:** current category Web preference and transient/persistent mode remain authoritative for recovered toasts. Existing category Sound preference remains authoritative; recovery sound is rate-limited to one per listener per 30 seconds so resumed events do not create a stale audio burst. Transient still expires after the established 4.5 seconds; persistent remains persistent. Disabled categories are advanced without a toast/sound. If no Web/Sound channel is enabled, the cursor advances locally so events created while opted out are not replayed after re-enabling.
- **Failure visibility:** read errors, thrown network/authentication failures, malformed/over-limit pages, and cursor-storage failures return failure without advancing past the last saved event. A malformed saved cursor is safely replaced. The listener exposes connection/recovery status, a retry action for failed/incomplete recovery, and sanitized status-only console diagnostics. Repeated reconnect recovery is single-flight with one queued rerun. Each page is capped at 25 rows and each pass presents at most five events; one additional one-row probe distinguishes a full five-event backlog from a larger backlog. The recovery age bound is 15 minutes; no Push delivery is created.
- **Cross-tab correction:** the previous BroadcastChannel-only in-memory claim race was reproduced in two same-origin Chrome tabs: both claims could win before the message arrived. The new listener uses Web Locks to serialize a per-staff/per-event claim and persists a bounded (500 entries, 24-hour TTL) claim set in same-origin local storage; its in-memory cache is also capped at 500 IDs. The browser regression observed exactly one winner across tabs, a separate staff ID could claim the same event independently, and refresh did not replay the claim or recovery cursor. This was an unauthenticated local browser harness; no authenticated DEV event was generated. Browser targets without Web Locks/storage retain the old best-effort fallback and log that atomic cross-tab protection is unavailable.
- **Fresh Pick Push navigation inspection:** the Push protocol and `public/sw.js` both map the reminder to `/customer-operations/fresh-picks`. The route exists, requires staff authentication, and its workspace capability includes Owner, Manager, Customer Operations, and Bakery. The service worker accepts same-origin paths only and navigates/focuses an existing same-origin window or opens the target. Middleware redirects unauthenticated staff to `/login?next=...`; post-login resolution preserves the path only when the role may access it. The client Push registration reuses an existing service-worker registration and awaits `ready`, but does not explicitly call `registration.update()`; an installed PWA may therefore have an older active worker until its normal update check/activation. No source route defect was confirmed; the earlier iPhone Home result remains inconclusive between old worker state and the user session/role path. No worker change or new Push was made.

## 2026-11-12 — Recovery correctness review and DEV Preview release gate

- **Review corrections:** the recovered-event cursor is validated before any page is processed: every row must have a UUID event ID, fall within the requested 15-minute/current-time interval, and be strictly ordered after the previous cursor. Malformed or unordered pages fail visibly without delivering or advancing beyond the prior processed row. A cursor ahead of current time is reset to the bounded lookback start; the reset is persisted explicitly so monotonic cursor storage cannot keep the invalid future value. A cursor older than the 15-minute window is clamped, and the “older notifications skipped” warning remains visible across incomplete retries and refresh for up to 24 hours.
- **Backlog behavior:** reads remain RLS-protected using the signed-in browser client and active-staff SELECT policy. No service-role client, polling loop, event producer, Push dispatcher, or extra delivery is introduced. Query bounds are `(created_at,id)`, pages are at most 25 rows, each pass presents at most five, and a one-row probe distinguishes an exact five-event backlog from a remaining backlog. The cursor advances only after presentation succeeds and the cursor save succeeds. Remaining events retain their place for explicit Retry; query, authentication, presentation, ordering, cursor-storage, and over-limit failures show a visible recovery error. Events older than 15 minutes are deliberately unrecoverable and disclosed; the bounded window is not represented as complete historical recovery.
- **Preferences and sound:** the current staff/category preferences at recovery time remain authoritative. Transient Web toasts remain 4.5 seconds; persistent Web toasts remain indefinite with their action. Disabled categories do not present. Recovery coalesces sound to at most one cue per bounded pass, selecting a warning-tone category over informational categories when both are eligible; a category with Sound disabled cannot win. This prevents a routine earlier event from masking a later warning category and prevents a backlog sound burst. All eligible Web toasts remain individually displayed. Abrupt browser termination between presenting a toast and persisting its claim can cause a duplicate on a later recovery; this intentionally favors not silently losing an unpresented alert.
- **Cross-tab claims:** normal browsers use Web Locks with bounded per-staff/event claims persisted for 24 hours. Browsers without Web Locks use a bounded IndexedDB read/write transaction for same-origin tab coordination. Claiming happens after presentation; a failed presentation is retryable. If IndexedDB is unavailable/blocked, a same-tab/BroadcastChannel fallback still presents and logs that atomic cross-tab protection degraded; that rare fallback cannot promise cross-tab suppression. The legacy BroadcastChannel race remains reproducible in the test harness; Web Locks and IndexedDB each produce one same-staff presentation, keep different-staff claims independent, persist across reload, and allow retry after presentation failure.
- **Initial connection and overlap:** the recovery boundary is captured when the listener mounts and persisted before Realtime subscription; this covers events created between mount and `SUBSCRIBED`. Catch-up runs after each `SUBSCRIBED`, including reconnect. Live and recovery paths share the same staff/event claim, so overlapping delivery is suppressed. The cursor is not advanced by a failed/invalid recovery read. No hosted authenticated Realtime interruption/reconnect test was performed in this source-only qualification.
- **Local qualification:** recovery tests PASS, including five-item cursor boundary, more-than-five continuation, exact page boundary, same-timestamp UUID boundary, unordered/invalid data, future/aged cursors, persisted skipped-window notice, query/presentation/cursor failures, bounded limits, transient/persistent semantics, and RLS source-policy checks. Sound, Web/event, Push/New Order, waiting-list, and existing Email-freeze regressions PASS. Same-origin two-tab Chrome harness PASS; it reproduces the legacy race and verifies Web Locks, IndexedDB fallback, different-staff isolation, reload persistence, failure retry, and degraded no-lock/no-IDB reporting. TypeScript, focused ESLint, Prettier, `git diff --check`, and production build PASS.
