## 2026-11-12 — Hosted live failure narrowed to Realtime payload normalization

- **Deployed-source identity:** confirmed the listener implementation in commit `742fbb546d527bef4f0b5192021d0e44a43ba13e` matches the local inspected `StaffNotificationListener.tsx`.
- **Failure stage confirmed:** the exact banner is set only after the Realtime INSERT callback calls `toNotificationEvent(payload.new)` and gets `null` because `row.id` is absent or `row.code` is not in the notification catalogue (`StaffNotificationListener.tsx:75-78, 314-326`). Therefore the callback received a change and normalization rejected its row. Category preferences, deduplication claim, toast/sound presentation, and recovery-cursor persistence are not reached by that failing callback. It returns only from that callback; it does not close the channel, so a later eligible callback can still run.
- **Automatic retry behavior:** the unsupported-live-row branch does not call `runRecovery`; its “Try recovery again” path is manual. Automatic recovery does run after a channel reports `SUBSCRIBED` and after a presentation exception. In this test, the Owner manually selected Retry. The synthetic notification then appeared, and the listener reached its normal completed-recovery UI state; this supports a successful RLS read, preference evaluation, dedupe/presentation and cursor save for that recovery pass. It is not proof that the original live payload was valid or that it was successfully presented live. Sound on the manual recovery remains unconfirmed.
- **DEV publication/RLS checks:** for project `tzwtpxdcggesgjaqxkqr`, `staff_notification_events` is included in `supabase_realtime`; RLS is enabled; `authenticated` has SELECT privilege; and the active-staff SELECT policy exists. The client subscribes to INSERT on that table without a client-side filter, leaving row access to RLS. The exact event row has a non-null UUID and supported code. A bounded 15-minute aggregate found one event and zero missing/unsupported codes. These checks do not reveal the browser’s raw payload or prove its `SUBSCRIBED` callback timing.
- **Warning interpretation:** source sets the persistent 15-minute warning when the browser-stored recovery cursor predates the lookback boundary. This is evidence of a stale local cursor/lookback clamp, not by itself evidence that malformed or eligible event rows older than the window were found. The one recent test event was recovered.
- **Unresolved cause:** because the stored row is valid and recoverable, the evidence points to a live `payload.new` shape/field mismatch, but the actual payload was not captured. No Realtime log source was present in the bounded Supabase log result. Do not assert whether the missing field was `id`, `code`, or another upstream shape issue without the browser evidence.
- **Minimal candidate correction (not implemented):** capture only safe shape flags (`idPresent`, `codeSupported`) when normalization rejects a live payload and invoke the existing bounded RLS recovery path automatically, retaining visible failure/backlog status if recovery cannot complete. No database or RLS/publication change is indicated. Before implementation, obtain a sanitized Safari Realtime status/payload-shape capture from the existing test session. Never copy full WebSocket URLs, headers, cookies, tokens, or payloads into chat.

## 2026-11-12 — DEV notification recovery continuation diagnosis and local correction

- **Scope and safety:** local-only work on `/private/tmp/project-flour-staff-notifications-20261109`, branch `codex/staff-notifications-dev-20261109`, starting commit `78777767b4796a579896892e6ae67fcd2b8d2460`. No hosted event was created during this investigation; no database, migration, Vercel setting, deployment, schedule, preference, Push or Email operation occurred. Production and `dev.whitebird.asia` were not accessed.
- **Six-event evidence:** the six test rows share `2026-11-12 06:25:52.230974+00 UTC`. The recovery query orders by `created_at ASC, id ASC`; actual order was event labels `01, 04, 02, 05, 03, 06`. Event `cfacd88a-6bc0-4784-9c50-ed5a22a544ae` (label `06`) was therefore the sixth row in the recovery order. The Owner observed five popups and Retry after reopening. Individual popup/event-ID mapping was not captured. A read-back about 3m25s after insertion confirmed all six were then within the 15-minute window. During the later Wi-Fi attempt the Safari page became inaccessible; after reconnect the Owner saw no sixth popup or Retry and the old-window warning. The later retry timestamp and Realtime status were not captured. The sixth event was **not observed as recovered**, but evidence does not establish whether it was permanently lost, excluded after aging past the 15-minute window, or merely not observed.
- **Recovery/cursor review:** the recovery query is RLS-protected and bounded to the 15-minute window, ordered by `(created_at, id)`, with page size 25 and at most five presentations per run. The cursor is saved after each successful presentation; after presentation five, a one-row probe determines whether the result is `incomplete` while leaving the cursor at event five. A failed fetch, presentation, or cursor save returns `failed` without moving the cursor beyond the last successfully processed event. Claims are staff-and-event scoped and committed only after the presentation callback succeeds; an already-claimed event is treated as handled by the winning same-staff tab. No source evidence showed the cursor advancing over the unpresented sixth event.
- **Confirmed defect:** the listener transitioned to `recovery-incomplete` after the five-item cap, which exposed Retry. A subsequent Realtime `CHANNEL_ERROR`, `TIMED_OUT`, or `CLOSED` callback unconditionally changed the same state to `disconnected` and replaced its recovery message. The Retry button was rendered only for `recovery-failed` or `recovery-incomplete`, so disconnect could hide the only manual continuation action. The existing older-than-15-minute message is persisted separately for up to 24 hours and can appear without proving that these six events expired.
- **Minimal local correction:** added a staff-scoped localStorage marker for pending/failed recovery. It is set for incomplete or failed recovery and cleared only after a complete recovery. On listener setup, the marker is read; the listener's subscription callback restores the retry state, avoiding synchronous React state updates in an effect. Realtime disconnection now preserves a clear recent-backlog/retry message (and retains the older-window notice when applicable). Retry remains visible while recovery is pending, including across a disconnected state, and is hidden during an active recovery. The recovery cursor, query window/order, five-item cap, preferences, transient/persistent presentation, Sound throttling, and Push/Email behavior were not changed. The marker contains no event or customer data and is keyed only by staff ID.
- **Automated qualification:** recovery suite PASS, including exactly five, six-event continuation (cursor stays at event five and a second pass delivers only event six), 24-event multi-batch continuation, temporary offline failure after the first batch, persisted pending state across simulated refresh, retry after reconnect, marker clearance after completion, cursor-save and presentation failure behavior, lookback boundary, RLS policy source, and preference/presentation contracts. Live-fallback PASS; Sound PASS; Fresh Pick hold-reminder PASS; staff-notification regression PASS; Push regression PASS. Same-origin headless browser test PASS: reproduced the legacy BroadcastChannel-only race, and verified exactly-once same-staff claims with Web Locks and IndexedDB fallback, other-staff independence, persistence after reload, and retry after presentation failure. TypeScript, focused ESLint, Prettier, and `git diff --check` PASS.
- **Build:** the default restricted build attempt could not fetch the repository's existing Newsreader and Outfit Google Fonts. The same unchanged `npm run build` then ran on the Mac host with network access and exited 0; compilation, TypeScript, and all 47 static pages completed. Existing warning only: Next.js deprecates the `middleware` filename convention in favor of `proxy`; it is unrelated to these changes.
- **Runtime conclusion and limits:** the source-level UI defect is confirmed and corrected locally. The exact reason the sixth event did not appear after the Owner's later reconnect remains unresolved because the browser's event-specific cursor, recovery outcome, `SUBSCRIBED` status, and timing relative to the 15-minute cutoff were not captured. The event was not proven permanently lost or skipped while still eligible. No Safari runtime acceptance has been repeated.
- **Candidate state:** local changes are uncommitted and not published. Files changed for this correction: `src/components/shell/StaffNotificationListener.tsx`, `src/foundation/staff/staff-notification-recovery.ts`, `scripts/test-staff-notification-recovery.ts`, and this append-only status document. There is no schema change or migration. The candidate is locally qualified for separate review, not hosted deployment. Minimal future hosted acceptance: with one already-authorized DEV receiver and Push/Email off, create no new events; use the existing six-event backlog only if events remain within 15 minutes, verify the pending Retry survives a controlled disconnect/refresh, and confirm continuation shows only remaining eligible events. If the events have expired, do not claim acceptance from them; a new coordinated test requires separate authorization.

## 2026-11-12 — Diagnosis of hosted recovery continuation inconclusive result

- **DEV evidence:** read-only recheck against DEV project `tzwtpxdcggesgjaqxkqr` returned the same six event rows, all `fresh_pick_walk_in_hold_reminder`, all created at `2026-11-12 07:24:58.451221 UTC` (`15:24:58.451221 Asia/Kuching`), each with zero Push and zero Email delivery rows. At `15:34:36 MYT`, the rows were still inside the 15-minute recovery window, whose boundary was approximately `15:39:58.451 MYT`. The Owner's Retry-disappearance/reconnect observation was recorded by about `15:30:39 MYT`, also before expiry. The cutoff therefore does not explain the observed disappearance of Retry.
- **Recovery lifecycle in the deployed source:** on each Realtime `SUBSCRIBED` callback, the listener immediately invokes bounded recovery. It sets `recoveryRunning` and state `recovering`, during which Retry is intentionally hidden. A successful complete result clears the staff-scoped pending marker and Retry; an incomplete/failed result sets the marker and should make Retry visible after the run stops. Realtime disconnect preserves the pending marker/message, and the component lives in the authenticated `AppShellFrame` layout; network-status callbacks alone do not prove a React remount. A full navigation/remount remains possible but was not observed in this test. No event-specific `SUBSCRIBED`, recovery-result, or cursor telemetry was captured, so the exact transition that hid Retry is unresolved.
- **Presentation behavior:** `ToastProvider` auto-dismisses transient toasts after 4.5 seconds and retains at most five visible; adding a sixth drops the oldest visible toast. Therefore five popups disappearing is expected, and a sixth delivered during an automatic continuation could replace an earlier toast while the visible count remains five. The Owner reported five initially and later all popups gone, but could not identify a separate sixth. The source's recovery query orders the six same-timestamp rows by UUID: `37c14656-791c-4feb-9d96-129029ecd50a`, `3a6f615e-2627-435b-9f64-549aeb38a3bd`, `4139678a-1995-4bd9-a34e-b27d9afd7efd`, `6d583c68-8025-4f9e-9627-8c3dc2ee36f5`, `9fdaa3fb-3369-46f8-b4b2-c2f898285383`, then `d9eba259-d2ab-45f1-b640-e0f00e5de15e` (test label 06). The first recovery pass's cursor, second-pass cursor, and claim state were not read from the Owner's browser, so the sixth is not distinguishable from a completed-but-unobserved toast using current server-side DEV evidence.
- **Confirmed issue and limits:** no recovery data loss or premature cursor advancement is proven. There is a confirmed observability gap: DEV exposes event rows and Push/Email deliveries, but Web presentation claims/cursor are browser-local and the listener logs only channel status/generic recovery errors, not event-ID-level recovery outcomes. The Retry control being absent after reconnect is consistent with successful automatic completion, but does not prove it. No local source correction was made; there is no identified algorithm defect beyond insufficient event-level acceptance evidence.
- **Minimal deterministic improvement before another hosted test:** add a bounded, local-only diagnostic for the last six (or fewer) recovery event IDs with state transitions `eligible`, `presentation queued`, `toast retained/rendered`, `cursor saved`, `pending`, and a sanitized completion reason (`complete`, `more_due`, `fetch_failed`, `present_failed`, `cursor_save_failed`, `expired`). Include timestamps, remaining-count, pending-marker state, Retry visibility reason, and Realtime status. Store no title, description, order/customer data, staff ID in values, credentials, or network URLs; namespace local storage by authenticated staff as existing cursor state does, cap/expire records, and expose only to the authenticated same-origin DEV acceptance session. Instrument ToastProvider retention/rendering so `toast queued` is not falsely described as visible. This is proposed only; no code, deployment, migration, extra logging, or hosted test was performed.
- **Next acceptance method:** use the same six existing rows only while within the 15-minute window; collect the sanitized client diagnostic before and after reconnect; compare its ordered event-ID states with the six known IDs and local cursor. A `cursor saved` entry after the sixth plus `toast retained/rendered` distinguishes processing from a still-pending sixth, while the recovery outcome and cutoff explain Retry visibility. Do not create another event unless this evidence expires and the Owner separately authorizes a new test.

## 2026-11-12 — Local-only deterministic recovery diagnostics

- **Scope:** local-only implementation in the notification branch worktree, starting from commit `c50efefef3107bdef3e983bbdaa3aea54d0199b4`. No commit, push, deployment, migration, hosted event, database read/write, schedule activation, Push/Email delivery, or preference change was made for this work. - **Diagnostic activation boundary:** enabled only for an authenticated Master Owner when Vercel runtime metadata says Preview, project `prj_3rmlAQzLSFOi15DEGczSuDYDq8xr`, branch `codex/staff-notifications-dev-20261109`, and the exact DEV Supabase URL `https://tzwtpxdcggesgjaqxkqr.supabase.co`. The browser must also be on the deployment-generated `VERCEL_URL` hostname, constrained to `.vercel.app`; aliases such as `dev.whitebird.asia`, Production, other projects/branches, non-Owner staff, and local development fail closed. Missing metadata disables the panel. Vercel documents `VERCEL_PROJECT_ID`, `VERCEL_ENV`, `VERCEL_GIT_COMMIT_REF`, and `VERCEL_URL` as system environment variables; the actual effective values for any future deployment must still be checked before hosted acceptance.
- **Local retention/privacy:** records are allowlisted and contain only millisecond timestamps, run IDs, opaque event UUIDs, source/status/outcome/reason enums, cursor UUID/time, counts and booleans. They contain no notification text, category, order/customer data, staff ID, URL, token, or arbitrary error. The trace is held in this tab's `sessionStorage`, capped at 200 records and one hour, and has an explicit Clear control. Writes are deferred and best-effort so storage failure cannot interrupt toast presentation or cursor updates. The panel is mounted only within the authenticated Master Owner layout and host-gated again in the browser. “Toast retained” means it remained in the five-item provider stack; “Toast mounted” means React mounted the DOM node. Neither is described as proof a person saw it.
- **Trace records:** `realtime_status` records sanitized channel transitions; `recovery_started` records page-load/reconnect/manual-retry/live-fallback/continuation source and run ID; `recovery_window` and `recovery_page` record cursor boundary and bounded page count; `event_eligible` distinguishes rows returned through the RLS query from staff preference eligibility; `normalization` records accepted/rejected shape only; `dedupe_claim` records claimed/duplicate/failure; `event_processing` records accepted/rejected/failure; `toast_queued`, `toast_retained`, `toast_mounted`, `toast_evicted`, `toast_dismissed`, and `toast_expired` distinguish queue, stack, DOM mount, limit eviction and expiry; `cursor_saved` records cursor ID/time and persistence result; `pending_marker` and `retry_visibility` record state and reason; `recovery_finished` records complete/incomplete/failed outcome and bounded count/cursor. These are client-side diagnostics only; no additional Supabase reads or server logs were added.
